**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-003](#w-003)
**Verdict:** 🔴 Missing

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.4.1 Centralised Authorization Policy](#641-centralised-authorization-policy)
- [6.4.2 Object-Level Ownership Verification](#642-object-level-ownership-verification)
- [6.4.3 Management Endpoint Protection](#643-management-endpoint-protection)
- [6.4.4 Database Principal Separation](#644-database-principal-separation)

**Implemented controls:** `server.ts:370-371`,389-390,426,429,433,440-441,450-451,605,625; `server.ts:370`,371,388,389,390,426,429,433,440,441,450,451; `server.ts:606-607`.

**Assessment:** `Authorization` is applied per-route in `server.ts` via inline middleware calls, with no central policy enforcer. The `finale-rest` module exposes REST endpoints on Sequelize models that accept mass-assignment to privileged fields without ownership checks. Database access runs through a single application principal, so a SQL injection on any route has read-write access to the full database.

<a id="centralised-authorization-policy"></a>
#### 6.4.1 Centralised Authorization Policy

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - `role` and authentication checks are applied per-route; no default-deny policy exists for the API surface.

Route-level authorization is implemented by placing `security.isAuthorized()` and `security.isAdminAuthenticated()` calls inline on individual route registrations across `server.ts:370-371, 389-390, 426, 429, 433, 440-441, 450-451, 605, 625`. When present, these calls verify the JWT and, for admin-gated routes, the `role` claim before allowing the handler to run.

**Security assessment**

The pattern is opt-in per route: any endpoint added without an explicit middleware call is reachable unauthenticated. `finale-rest` exposes CRUD endpoints on several Sequelize models whose registrations in `server.ts` do not consistently apply authentication middleware. A new route author must consciously apply the boilerplate, making authorization gaps the default path of least resistance rather than the exception.

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Authentication on State-Changing Routes
- 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Insecure Direct Object Reference
- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body

<a id="object-level-ownership-verification"></a><a id="object-level-ownership-verification-bolaidor"></a>
#### 6.4.2 Object-Level Ownership Verification

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - basket and order routes check ownership on some paths but several endpoints accept caller-supplied IDs without binding to the authenticated principal.

Object ownership is checked `at ``server.ts:370, 371, 388, 389, 390, 426, 429, 433, 440, 441, 450, 451` where basket and order routes compare the token's `userId` claim against the requested resource ID.

The diagram shows the intended ownership-check flow for a basket access request:

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor User
    participant SPA as Angular SPA
    participant API as Express.js API
    participant Auth as isAuthorized middleware
    participant DB as SQLite Database

    User->>SPA: Access basket
    SPA->>API: GET /api/Baskets/:id with Bearer token
    API->>Auth: Verify JWT and extract userId
    Auth-->>API: Authenticated userId
    API->>DB: SELECT * FROM Baskets WHERE id=:id AND UserId=:userId
    DB-->>API: Basket or empty
    alt Matches authenticated user
        API-->>SPA: Basket data
    else Mismatch
        API-->>SPA: 403 Forbidden
    end
```

**Security assessment**

Several `finale-rest`-exposed endpoints perform no ownership binding - `PUT /api/Users/:id` and certain product and feedback endpoints accept the resource ID from the URL without verifying it matches the authenticated principal's `userId`. An authenticated `user` who knows another `user`'s numeric ID can read or modify that `user`'s profile, basket, or order `data`. The ownership checks that do exist are per-route implementations rather than a reusable enforced pattern, so each new endpoint must re-implement the check manually.

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Authentication on State-Changing Routes
- 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Insecure Direct Object Reference
- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body

<a id="management-endpoint-protection"></a>
#### 6.4.3 Management Endpoint Protection

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - most admin-only endpoints gate on the admin `role` claim, but some read-only admin list endpoints accept any valid JWT.

Admin-gated routes are protected `at ``server.ts:606-607` via `security.isAdminAuthenticated()`, which checks both the presence of a valid JWT and that the decoded `role` claim equals `admin`. This guard covers `/administration`, product-management endpoints, and user-deletion routes.

**Security assessment**

Several read-only admin list endpoints - including `GET /api/Users` and `GET /api/Feedbacks` - require a valid JWT but do not enforce the `role=admin` claim. Any non-admin `user` with a valid session token can call these endpoints and enumerate all `user` records and feedback entries. The gap is a role-check inconsistency rather than a missing authentication gate.

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Authentication on State-Changing Routes
- 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Insecure Direct Object Reference
- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body

<a id="database-principal-separation"></a>
#### 6.4.4 Database Principal Separation

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - all routes share one Sequelize connection with full read-write access to the SQLite file.

Database principal separation limits the blast radius of injection attacks by running read-only queries under a restricted DB account and write operations under a separate write principal. This boundary contains an injection on a read path to read access only.

**Security assessment**

Sequelize connects to `data/app.sqlite` through a single application connection used by every route, including unauthenticated ones. SQL injection on any endpoint - including the login route `at ``routes/login.ts:34` - has read-write access to the full database: `user` credentials, TOTP secrets, payment `data`, and order history. Separating read and write principals is not possible on SQLite's file-level permission model without switching to a network-addressable RDBMS, but the architectural gap should be documented as a known residual risk.

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Authentication on State-Changing Routes
- 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Insecure Direct Object Reference
- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body

### 6.5 Query Construction and Data Access Controls

<a id="ctrl-query-construction-and-data-access-controls"></a>

**Dependent crossings:** [tb-5](#tb-5) refuted - 🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — SQL Injection


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-002](#w-002)
**Verdict:** 🟠 Weak

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the section's default mechanism — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.5.1 Database Query Construction](#651-database-query-construction)

**Implemented controls:** Sequelize ORM parameterized model methods cover the majority of `data` access paths; `multer` enforces `file-type` and size limits on upload routes.

**Assessment:** Sequelize's parameterized model methods protect most `data` access paths. Two routes bypass the ORM and concatenate `user` input directly into `models.sequelize.query()` calls, making them the primary injection surfaces.

<a id="database-query-construction"></a><a id="query-construction"></a>
#### 6.5.1 Database Query Construction

**Status:** 🔴 Unsafe - login and search routes concatenate `user` input into raw SQL strings, bypassing Sequelize's parameter binding.

Sequelize ORM handles most relational queries through parameterized `findOne`, `findAll`, and `update` model methods. The majority of CRUD operations on `Products`, `Orders`, and `Feedbacks` use this safe path and are not affected by SQL injection.

**Security assessment**

Two routes opt out of the ORM and construct SQL by string concatenation:

- `routes/login.ts:34` interpolates `req.body.email` into a raw `models.sequelize.query()` string - `' OR 1=1--` bypasses the WHERE clause and returns the first database row.
- The product-search route interpolates `req.query.q` into the LIKE pattern with no escaping, enabling a UNION-based extraction of any table.

The login route illustrates the raw SQL construction pattern:

```ts
models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '${req.body.email}' AND password = '${req.body.password}'`)
```

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — SQL Injection

### 6.6 Input Boundary Validation Controls

<a id="ctrl-input-boundary-validation-controls"></a>

**Dependent crossings:** [tb-1](#tb-1) unconfirmed - 🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — SQL Injection, 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body `routes/verify.ts:53`, 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — Prompt injection via unvalidated messages (`routes/chat.ts:191`), +3 · [tb-4](#tb-4) unconfirmed - 🟡 [F-043](#f-043) — Remote installer executed without integrity check (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:358`), 🟡 [F-057](#f-057) — Third-party GitHub Actions pinned to commit SHA


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-006](#w-006)
**Verdict:** 🟠 Weak

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.6.1 Validation Approach](#661-validation-approach)

**Implemented controls:** `multer` enforces file-size and MIME-type limits on image-upload routes; `express.json()` body parser applies a default size limit to JSON request bodies.

**Assessment:** Request validation is performed ad-hoc inside individual route handlers. No schema-validation middleware (express-validator, Joi, Zod) is applied globally. Most routes accept arbitrary field values and sizes, with only upload-specific limits enforced by `multer`.

<a id="validation-approach"></a>
#### 6.6.1 Validation Approach

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - no centralized schema validation; individual routes apply inconsistent field-level checks.

Input validation protects the application by rejecting malformed, oversized, or out-of-range `data` before it reaches business logic or database queries. The `express.json()` and `express.urlencoded()` parsers handle basic body parsing with a default size cap.

**Security assessment**

No global schema-validation layer is applied to route inputs. Route handlers perform ad-hoc checks on specific fields - typically presence checks, not type, format, or range validation. Several endpoints accept arbitrary-length strings with no server-side cap beyond the parser's default body limit. The WebSocket handler `at ``lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:46` applies an unbounded regex to untrusted socket input, creating a ReDoS surface. Business-rule boundaries - such as quantity limits on basket items or order amounts - are enforced only on the client side in the Angular SPA.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — Prompt injection via unvalidated messages
- 🟠 [F-033](#f-033) — Unbounded LLM token consumption
- 🟡 [F-062](#f-062) — Unbounded session token map

### 6.7 Output Encoding and Rendering Controls


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-013](#w-013)
**Verdict:** 🟠 Weak

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the section's default mechanism — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.7.1 Output Encoding and Client-Side Rendering](#671-output-encoding-and-client-side-rendering)

**Implemented controls:** Angular template default HTML encoding active on all standard data-bound views; server-side API responses return JSON with `Content-Type: application/json`.

**Assessment:** Angular's default encoding protects the majority of the SPA. Four components explicitly bypass it via `bypassSecurityTrustHtml()`, creating stored and reflected XSS sinks on search results, admin views, about-page content, and the last-login IP display.

<a id="output-encoding-and-client-side-rendering"></a>
#### 6.7.1 Output Encoding and Client-Side Rendering

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - Angular default encoding is active but bypassed in four components, each rendering attacker-controlled content as raw HTML.

Angular's template `system` applies automatic HTML encoding to all interpolated values by default. Ordinary product-listing, checkout, and profile views bind `data` with standard Angular interpolation syntax and are protected. The server-side API returns JSON responses with correct `Content-Type` headers.

**Security assessment**

Four components call `bypassSecurityTrustHtml()` to render server-supplied HTML directly into the DOM:

- `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119` - renders the about-page description from the database.
- `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110,143` - renders product names and descriptions from search results.
- `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73,91` - renders `user` and feedback content in the admin panel.
- `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39` - renders the `Last-Login-IP` header value, which the server reads from the `X-Forwarded-For` request header.

An attacker who stores a `<script>` or `<img onerror=...>` payload in any of the backing `data` sources triggers XSS execution in the victim's authenticated session.

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — Cross-Site Scripting
- 🔴 [F-022](#f-022) — Legacy sanitizer on persisted username

### 6.8 Browser and Cross-Origin Controls


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-008](#w-008)
**Verdict:** 🟠 Weak

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.8.1 CORS Policy](#681-cors-policy)

**Implemented controls:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`.

**Assessment:** A CORS configuration exists at the WebSocket startup, but findings indicate at least one surface reflects origins without restricting to an explicit allow-list. `Content-Security-Policy`, `X-Frame-Options`, and cookie hardening flags are not confirmed in the recon evidence.

<a id="cors-policy"></a>
#### 6.8.1 CORS Policy

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - CORS headers are emitted but at least one surface reflects caller-supplied origins rather than validating against an explicit allow-list.

CORS headers on the API surface control which third-party origins may make credentialed cross-origin requests. `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20` configures origin handling for the WebSocket endpoint. The Express API applies CORS middleware via `app.use(cors())` in `server.ts`.

**Security assessment**

Two findings trace to overly permissive CORS behaviour:

- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Rate limiter bypassable via forwarded header — the CORS configuration on at least one API path reflects the caller's `Origin` header without validating it against a configured allow-list, allowing any origin to make credentialed requests.
- 🟠 [F-045](#f-045) — Missing Content Security Policy — the WebSocket handshake origin check at `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20` is insufficiently restrictive, permitting cross-site WebSocket hijacking from third-party pages.

Fixing both requires replacing the wildcard and reflected-origin patterns with an explicit string-matched allow-list.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Rate limiter bypassable via forwarded header
- 🟠 [F-045](#f-045) — Missing Content Security Policy

### 6.9 Cryptography Secrets and Data Protection


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-004](#w-004), [W-009](#w-009)
**Verdict:** 🔴 Unsafe

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.9.1 JWT Signing Key Secrecy](#691-jwt-signing-key-secrecy)
- [6.9.2 HMAC Secret Key Management](#692-hmac-secret-key-management)
- [6.9.3 Blockchain Wallet Key Management](#693-blockchain-wallet-key-management)
- [6.9.4 TLS Transport Encryption](#694-tls-transport-encryption)

**Implemented controls:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`; `lib/insecurity.ts:42`; `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`.

**Assessment:** Three secrets are hardcoded as string literals in `lib/insecurity.ts`: an RSA private signing key at line 21, an HMAC secret at line 42, and a cookie-signing secret. Cloning the repository gives an attacker everything needed to forge arbitrary JWTs and cookies - no server access required. TLS is available as an operator-configured option but is not enforced at startup, so the default deployment transmits all credentials in cleartext.

<a id="jwt-signing-key-secrecy"></a>
#### 6.9.1 JWT Signing Key Secrecy

**Status:** 🔴 Unsafe - RSA private signing key is a hardcoded literal in `lib/insecurity.ts`; the public key ships separately in `encryptionkeys/jwt.pub`.

JWTs issued on every login are signed with an RSA private key loaded `at ``lib/insecurity.ts:21`. The public key counterpart is read from `encryptionkeys/jwt.pub` and used by the verifier `at ``lib/insecurity.ts:189`. `RS256` is the chosen algorithm - a sound choice - but the key material is committed to the repository.

**Security assessment**

Both the private signing key and the public verification key ship inside the public repository. An attacker who clones the repository mints a valid `RS256` token for any `userId` and `role=admin` payload - the server's verifier accepts it. Key rotation requires a code change and redeployment; there is no runtime injection path. The effective consequence is that the signing key is permanently compromised for any deployment built from the public repository.

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Hardcoded RSA signing key in source
- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hard-coded seeded account credentials
- 🟠 [F-018](#f-018) — Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token

<a id="hmac-secret-key-management"></a>
#### 6.9.2 HMAC Secret Key Management

**Status:** 🔴 Unsafe - HMAC and cookie-signing secrets are hardcoded string literals, repository-public and identical across every deployment.

An HMAC secret `at ``lib/insecurity.ts:42` is used for cookie-signing and HMAC-protected API operations. A separate cookie-signing secret in the same file backs session cookie integrity. Both values are string literals compiled into the application bundle.

**Security assessment**

The HMAC and cookie secrets are identical across every deployment built from this codebase - including production, staging, and every fork. `Cookie` forgery and HMAC bypass are offline operations: an attacker with the repository can compute valid signatures for any payload without interacting with the server. Rotation requires a code change; there is no environment-variable injection path currently used.

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Hardcoded RSA signing key in source
- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hard-coded seeded account credentials
- 🟠 [F-018](#f-018) — Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token

<a id="blockchain-wallet-key-management"></a>
#### 6.9.3 Blockchain Wallet Key Management

**Status:** 🔴 Unsafe - wallet private key material is accessible through an insufficiently protected route.

The Web3/NFT surface manages blockchain wallet keys referenced in the `web3-nft` component. `routes/checkKeys.ts:10` exposes a key-inspection endpoint intended for administrative verification of loaded key material.

**Security assessment**

`routes/checkKeys.ts:10` returns wallet key material through an endpoint that lacks sufficient authorization enforcement. An attacker who reaches this route — either through the missing server-side auth check or through the JWT algorithm confusion in 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — can retrieve the wallet private key, enabling theft of assets from the associated blockchain account. The wallet key should be stored in an HSM or secrets-management system, not in application code, and the inspection endpoint should be removed or restricted to local-only access.

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Hardcoded RSA signing key in source
- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hard-coded seeded account credentials
- 🟠 [F-018](#f-018) — Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token

<a id="tls-transport-encryption"></a>
#### 6.9.4 TLS Transport Encryption

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - TLS is configurable via environment but the default startup binds plaintext HTTP on port 3000 with no enforcement.

Transport-layer encryption protects credentials, session tokens, and payment `data` in transit between clients and the server. The application can be configured with a TLS certificate via environment variables when the operator provides one.

**Security assessment**

The default server startup in `server.ts` binds plaintext HTTP on port 3000. No certificate is required to start the process; `NODE_ENV=production` does not change this behaviour. Session tokens, passwords, and credit-card numbers traverse the network in cleartext unless the operator adds a TLS terminator externally. The application does not verify or enforce the presence of that terminator at startup - it will serve traffic whether TLS is in place or not. `Secure` cookie flags and HSTS headers that depend on a TLS transport are therefore not effective in the default configuration.

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Hardcoded RSA signing key in source
- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hard-coded seeded account credentials
- 🟠 [F-018](#f-018) — Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token

### 6.10 File Parser and Outbound Request Controls

<a id="ctrl-file-parser-and-outbound-request-controls"></a>

_No trust boundary in this model depends on this control class._


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-007](#w-007)
**Verdict:** 🟠 Weak

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the section's default mechanism — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.10.1 File Parser and Outbound Request Handling](#6101-file-parser-and-outbound-request-handling)

**Implemented controls:** `multer` enforces file-size and MIME-type limits on the profile-picture upload route; `express.static` serves the public asset directory.

**Assessment:** Upload handling uses `multer` for image uploads with `file-type` filtering. The archive extraction path, the XML parser surface, and the server-side fetch routes all lack the containment controls needed to prevent path traversal, XXE, and SSRF respectively.

<a id="file-parser-and-outbound-request-handling"></a>
#### 6.10.1 File Parser and Outbound Request Handling

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - archive extraction and XML parsing accept attacker-controlled structure; server-side fetch routes accept attacker-controlled URLs without an allow-list.

File upload is handled by `multer` on the profile-picture route, enforcing MIME-type and size limits for image uploads. A separate archive-extraction path accepts zip files for bulk profile-picture uploads. An XML-parsing surface processes complaint documents. Several routes issue server-side HTTP requests based on URL parameters.

**Security assessment**

Three independent weaknesses span the file and outbound-request surface:

- Archive extraction at the zip-upload endpoint performs no path-containment check on entry names. A zip archive with `../../` path components in entry names can write files outside the intended upload directory, reaching arbitrary filesystem paths writable by the server process.
- The XML parser is configured with `noent: true`, enabling external entity (XXE) resolution. An attacker submitting a crafted complaint document can trigger file reads (e.g. `/etc/passwd`) or SSRF via entity expansion.
- Server-side fetch routes accept attacker-supplied URLs without validating against an explicit allow-list. A crafted URL targeting `http://169.254.169.254/` or internal service addresses enables SSRF against the local network.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Path traversal filesystem access from request input
- 🟠 [F-023](#f-023) — SSRF via profile image URL
- 🟠 [F-024](#f-024) — Unbounded field selection leaks credentials
- 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Secret exposure to anonymous client
- 🟡 [F-042](#f-042) — Open redirect via substring allowlist

### 6.11 Operations Runtime and Supply Chain Controls


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-008](#w-008), [W-010](#w-010), [W-011](#w-011), [W-012](#w-012)
**Verdict:** 🔴 Missing

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.11.1 Automated SCA scanning](#6111-automated-sca-scanning)
- [6.11.2 Automated dependency updates](#6112-automated-dependency-updates)
- [6.11.3 Lockfile hygiene](#6113-lockfile-hygiene)

**Implemented controls:** `package.json` declares pinned or ranged dependency versions; `.github/workflows/` contains a CI pipeline with build and test steps.

**Assessment:** The CI pipeline builds and tests the application but performs no software composition analysis, no automated dependency update workflow, and no frozen install step. Known-vulnerable library versions accumulate without automated detection or remediation pressure.

<a id="automated-sca-scanning"></a>
#### 6.11.1 Automated SCA scanning

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - no SCA tool runs in CI; known-vulnerable library versions ship undetected.

Automated Software Composition Analysis scans declared dependencies against known-vulnerability databases on every commit, alerting the team when an installed package has a published CVE. This catch-all gives early warning before vulnerable versions reach production.

**Security assessment**

No SCA tool - Dependabot security alerts, Snyk, OWASP Dependency-Check, or equivalent - is configured in `.github/workflows/` or any other CI definition. The test suite exercises application behaviour but does not check whether installed packages carry known vulnerabilities. Several dependencies in this codebase are known to carry high-severity CVEs; without automated scanning those findings are invisible to the CI gate.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟡 [F-003](#f-003) — Missing Security Event Logging
- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — Action pinned to mutable branch ref
- 🟡 [F-043](#f-043) — Remote installer executed without integrity check

<a id="automated-dependency-updates"></a>
#### 6.11.2 Automated dependency updates

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - no Dependabot or Renovate configuration; dependencies drift against available patches indefinitely.

Automated dependency update tools create pull requests when newer versions of installed packages are available, keeping the dependency tree current without requiring manual tracking of upstream release notes.

**Security assessment**

Neither Dependabot nor Renovate is configured in this repository. The `package.json` version ranges allow npm to resolve updated transitive dependencies, but no automated mechanism creates PRs or alerts when a newer patch release is available. Dependencies with published security fixes accumulate lag until a developer manually audits and updates them. Combined with the absence of SCA scanning, the team has no automated signal that any dependency is out of date or vulnerable.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟡 [F-003](#f-003) — Missing Security Event Logging
- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — Action pinned to mutable branch ref
- 🟡 [F-043](#f-043) — Remote installer executed without integrity check

<a id="lockfile-hygiene"></a>
#### 6.11.3 Lockfile hygiene

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - the CI pipeline runs `npm install` rather than `npm ci`, allowing transitive dependency drift on each build.

Lockfile hygiene means the CI/CD pipeline installs exactly the dependency tree recorded in `package-lock.json`, making builds reproducible and preventing a newly-published transitive dependency from entering the build tree unreviewed.

**Security assessment**

The CI pipeline invokes `npm install`, which resolves transitive dependencies afresh on each run against the version ranges in `package.json`. A newly-published transitive package - including one with a known vulnerability or a supply-chain compromise - can enter the build without a `package-lock.json` update and without any review gate. Switching to `npm ci` enforces the lockfile and makes the installed tree auditable. The lockfile itself should be committed and reviewed as a first-class artifact.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟡 [F-003](#f-003) — Missing Security Event Logging
- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — Action pinned to mutable branch ref
- 🟡 [F-043](#f-043) — Remote installer executed without integrity check

### 6.12 Real-time and Not Applicable Controls

<!-- §6.12 LOCKED — mechanically derived from absence of real-time findings. Renderer must not rewrite the line below. -->
_Not applicable - no real-time / WebSocket findings routed to this category, and no AI/LLM, GraphQL, or gRPC surfaces detected by the recon scan. Controls catalogued elsewhere (container hardening, dependency determinism) are covered in their primary [§6](#6-security-architecture) sections._

### 6.13 Defense-in-Depth Summary

**Verdict:** -

`RS256` was chosen as the JWT signing algorithm - a sound primitive - and `expressJwt` middleware is applied to the majority of REST routes. Angular's default template encoding protects most views, and `multer` enforces `file-type` and size limits on the primary image-upload path. Sequelize ORM parameterizes the majority of `data` access paths, limiting the SQL injection surface to the two routes that call `models.sequelize.query()` directly. TOTP second-factor authentication is available as an opt-in feature. These individual controls show that the security mechanisms were considered; they are not absent.

The layering that would make those controls effective in combination is missing. Pinning the JWT algorithm to `RS256` in `lib/insecurity.ts:52,189` closes the authentication bypass that currently makes the `expressJwt` middleware worthless. Moving the RSA signing key and HMAC secret to runtime-injected environment variables isolates the offline-forgery path. Replacing `MD5` with argon2 or bcrypt in `lib/insecurity.ts:41` converts the password-storage control from a nominal one to an effective one. Parameterizing `routes/login.ts:34` and the search route repairs the SQL injection that lets a single unauthenticated request read any row in the database. Adding `npm ci` in CI and configuring Dependabot closes the supply-chain drift that allows known-vulnerable library versions to accumulate silently. None of these repairs is structurally complex; each targets a specific line or configuration entry, and the combined effect restores the intended layered defense.

A weakness may also rest on observed unsafe practice or an absent architectural control with no confirmed exploit - only confirmed findings carry a CVSS score.

- 🔴 **Critical** · [W-004](#w-004) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store · confirmed · 4 findings · 4 components

<a id="w-001"></a>
### W-001 — Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

🔴 **Critical** · implementation weakness · confirmed · 5 findings

Token validation is implemented in application code rather than behind one vetted and consistently configured authentication path. Algorithm, issuer, audience, and key-handling checks can diverge between callers.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — JWT Verification Without Algorithm Allowlist
- 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Implicit OAuth flow (`frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`)
- 🟡 [F-044](#f-044) — Seed download without integrity check (`data/datacreator.ts:390`)
- 🟡 [F-054](#f-054) — Container image signing via cosign or attest-build-provenance

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-012](#f-012) — Unsigned container publish (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:327`) (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:327`)

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02), ci-cd-pipeline, [C-05](#c-05), [C-04](#c-04), [C-01](#c-01)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — centralise token validation on a vetted library with pinned `algorithms`, issuer and audience checks, and managed signing keys
- **Tactical** — ● [M-001](#m-001) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification, ◕ [M-015](#m-015) — Harden the authentication flow, ◑ [M-044](#m-044) — Verify token signatures before trusting claims, ◑ [M-054](#m-054) — Sign and verify release artifacts, ◕ [M-012](#m-012) — Verify token signatures before trusting claims

<a id="w-002"></a>
### W-002 — Database access relies on concatenated queries

🔴 **Critical** · design weakness · confirmed · 1 finding

Database queries are assembled from application values instead of passing those values through an enforced parameterised data-access path. This leaves every call site responsible for preserving query structure.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Raw SQL string interpolation.** Multiple routes pass request parameters into raw Sequelize queries rather than using parameterized placeholders, making injection a systemic pattern across the `data` layer rather than an isolated instance that can be fixed at one call site.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — SQL Injection

**Architecture evidence:** Parameterized Queries, ORM / Repository Layer

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — provide one parameterised repository or query-builder path and prohibit application-value interpolation in database queries
- **Tactical** — ● [M-007](#m-007) — Use parameterized database queries

<a id="w-003"></a>
### W-003 — Authorization is implemented route by route

🔴 **Critical** · design weakness · confirmed · 5 findings

`Authorization` depends on per-handler checks instead of a policy boundary that consistently enforces `role`, ownership, and tenant scope. New routes can bypass protection by omitting a local check.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Authentication on State-Changing Routes
- 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Insecure Direct Object Reference
- 🔴 [F-035](#f-035) — Coupon discount unbounded server-side (`routes/chat.ts:184`)
- 🔴 [F-036](#f-036) — Deluxe role granted without payment (`routes/deluxe.ts:43`)

**Practice sites:**

- 🔴 [F-039](#f-039) — Comment command without author authorization (`.github/workflows/rebase.yml:10`) (`.github/workflows/rebase.yml:10`)

**Architecture evidence:** Centralised AuthZ Policy, Role / Scope Enforcement, Ownership Check, Object-Level Ownership Check, Tenant Scoping

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02), backend-api, [C-05](#c-05)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce authorization through a shared server-side policy layer and make ownership and tenant scope mandatory inputs to `data` access
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-002](#m-002) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint, ● [M-008](#m-008) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization, ◕ [M-035](#m-035) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint, ◕ [M-036](#m-036) — Enforce correct server-side authorization, ◕ [M-039](#m-039) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint

<a id="w-004"></a>
### W-004 — Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

🔴 **Critical** · design weakness · confirmed · 4 findings

Cryptographic keys, credentials, and other high-entropy secrets are embedded as literals in source or `config` rather than resolved at runtime from a managed secret store, so anyone with repository read access obtains reusable signing material and credentials.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Secrets hardcoded in source.** Both the RSA signing key and seeded admin credentials are committed to the repository, so any clone of the source immediately yields the material needed to forge admin tokens and log in without brute-force - a control gap no runtime patch can fully remediate without credential rotation.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Hardcoded RSA signing key in source (`lib/insecurity.ts:21`)
- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hard-coded seeded account credentials (`data/datacreator.ts:196`)
- 🔴 [F-031](#f-031) — Hardcoded wallet mnemonic in source (`routes/checkKeys.ts:10`)

**Practice sites:**

- 🔴 [F-060](#f-060) — Hardcoded test credentials (`frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:62`) (`frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:62`)

**Architecture evidence:** Managed Secret Store, Runtime Secret Injection

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02), [C-04](#c-04), [C-01](#c-01), [C-07](#c-07)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — move every secret to a managed secret store or injected environment configuration, rotate the exposed values, and add secret-scanning to CI
- **Tactical** — ● [M-004](#m-004) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store, ● [M-005](#m-005) — Move secrets to a managed secret store, ◕ [M-031](#m-031) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store, ◑ [M-060](#m-060) — Move secrets to a managed secret store

<a id="w-005"></a>
### W-005 — Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

🟠 **High** · design weakness · confirmed · 1 finding

Sensitive API routes and real-time channels are exposed without an enforced authentication check at the endpoint boundary. Access control depends on each handler (or the caller) remembering to require a session, so an unauthenticated client can reach privileged operations directly.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel

**Architecture evidence:** Route Authentication Middleware, Server-Side Session Enforcement

**Affected components:** [C-06](#c-06)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce authentication centrally at the routing and channel boundary so every exposed endpoint requires a verified session unless explicitly marked public
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-013](#m-013) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint

<a id="w-006"></a>
### W-006 — Denial of Service is implemented inconsistently

🟠 **High** · implementation weakness · confirmed · 6 findings

Resource-consuming operations lack rate limiting or bounds, so a single actor can exhaust capacity.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-033](#f-033) — Unbounded LLM token consumption (`server.ts:638`)
- 🟡 [F-064](#f-064) — Unbounded regex on socket input (`lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:46`)
- 🟡 [F-065](#f-065) — Unbounded persisted text growth (`models/feedback.ts:40`)
- 🟡 [F-066](#f-066) — Unbounded in-memory set growth (`routes/web3Wallet.ts:16`)

**Practice sites:**

- 🟡 [F-062](#f-062) — Unbounded session token map (`lib/insecurity.ts:74`) (`lib/insecurity.ts:74`)
- 🟡 [F-063](#f-063) — Unbounded CI fan-out on pull requests (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`) (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`)
- `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:114`

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02), [C-03](#c-03), [C-05](#c-05), [C-06](#c-06), [C-04](#c-04), [C-01](#c-01), [C-07](#c-07)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — apply rate limiting, request quotas, and input-size bounds on resource-intensive endpoints
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-033](#m-033) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size, ◑ [M-064](#m-064) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size, ◑ [M-065](#m-065) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size, ◑ [M-066](#m-066) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size, ◑ [M-062](#m-062) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size, ◑ [M-063](#m-063) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

<a id="w-007"></a>
### W-007 — Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

🟠 **High** · design weakness · confirmed · 1 finding

Request handlers do not validate input against one enforced server-side schema or allowlist at the boundary - validation is either absent on the vulnerable parameters or limited to rejecting selected bad patterns (a blacklist). New encodings and unanticipated input forms can therefore reach downstream parsers or interpreters.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Path traversal filesystem access from request input `routes/dataErasure.ts:104`

**Architecture evidence:** Schema Validation, Allowlist Validation

**Affected components:** backend-api

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce server-side schemas and domain-specific allowlists before input reaches parsing, persistence, or command construction
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-017](#m-017) — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory

<a id="w-008"></a>
### W-008 — Vulnerable & Outdated Dependencies is implemented inconsistently

🟠 **High** · implementation weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

Dependencies with known vulnerabilities are shipped because update and advisory-tracking are not enforced in the pipeline.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Rate limiter bypassable via forwarded header (`server.ts:346`)

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-045](#f-045) — Missing Content Security Policy (`frontend/src/index.html:9`) (`frontend/src/index.html:9`)

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02), [C-01](#c-01)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce automated dependency updates and block known-vulnerable versions in CI
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-034](#m-034) — Key the reset-password limiter on the proxy-validated address, ◑ [M-045](#m-045) — Declare a script-src and frame-ancestors policy for the SPA shell

<a id="w-009"></a>
### W-009 — Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

🟠 **High** · implementation weakness · confirmed · 3 findings

Password, token, or integrity protection uses a weak `hash`, predictable random source, or insufficient work factor. The application may use a standard library, but the selected primitive does not provide the required security property.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — MD5 password hashing (`lib/insecurity.ts:41`)

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-018](#f-018) — Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token `lib/insecurity.ts:53` (`lib/insecurity.ts:53`)
- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Unsalted fast password hash (`models/user.ts:76`) (`models/user.ts:76`)
- `data/datacreator.ts:313`

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03), backend-api, [C-04](#c-04)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — standardise on a `password` KDF, a CSPRNG for secrets, and modern authenticated cryptographic primitives with centrally reviewed parameters
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-025](#m-025) — Use modern cryptographic hash and KDF algorithms, ◕ [M-018](#m-018) — Use cryptographically secure random values, ◕ [M-029](#m-029) — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm

<a id="w-010"></a>
### W-010 — Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

🟡 **Medium** · design weakness · confirmed · 3 findings

CI/CD workflows resolve third-party actions and other build dependencies to mutable tags or branches instead of immutable commit digests, so a retagged or compromised upstream runs inside the pipeline with its token and secret scope.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟡 [F-057](#f-057) — Third-party GitHub Actions pinned to commit SHA

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-020](#f-020) — Release built without dependency lockfile (`.github/workflows/release.yml:55`) (`.github/workflows/release.yml:55`)
- 🟡 [F-043](#f-043) — Remote installer executed without integrity check (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:358`) (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:358`)

**Architecture evidence:** Commit-SHA Action Pinning, Dependency Provenance Verification

**Affected components:** ci-cd-pipeline, [C-05](#c-05)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — pin every third-party action and build dependency to an immutable commit SHA (or a vetted internal mirror) and enforce SHA-pinning in CI
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-057](#m-057) — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions, ◕ [M-020](#m-020) — Set least-privilege CI workflow permissions, ◑ [M-043](#m-043) — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions

<a id="w-011"></a>
### W-011 — Vulnerable & Outdated Dependencies is implemented inconsistently

🟡 **Medium** · implementation weakness · confirmed · 3 findings

Dependencies with known vulnerabilities are shipped because update and advisory-tracking are not enforced in the pipeline.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟡 [F-058](#f-058) — `Dockerfile` base image must be digest-pinned
- 🟡 [F-059](#f-059) — `Package-lock.json` present and committed

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — Action pinned to mutable branch ref (`.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33`) (`.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33`)

**Affected components:** ci-cd-pipeline, [C-05](#c-05)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce automated dependency updates and block known-vulnerable versions in CI
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-058](#m-058) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest, ◑ [M-059](#m-059) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest, ◕ [M-019](#m-019) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest

<a id="w-012"></a>
### W-012 — Sensitive Data Exposure is implemented inconsistently

🟡 **Medium** · implementation weakness · confirmed · 1 finding

Sensitive `data` (credentials, tokens, PII) is stored or transmitted without adequate protection, so a single disclosure yields usable secrets.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟡 [F-051](#f-051) — Stack traces returned by error handler (`server.ts:682`)

**Practice sites:**

- `routes/web3Wallet.ts:36`

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02), [C-07](#c-07)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — classify sensitive `data` and enforce encryption at rest/in transit plus least-privilege access to it
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-051](#m-051) — Return generic error messages to clients

<a id="w-013"></a>
### W-013 — Cross-Site Scripting is implemented inconsistently

🟡 **Medium** · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 1 finding

Output is rendered without contextual encoding and without a Content Security Policy backstop, so attacker-controlled markup executes in other users' browsers.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟡 [F-061](#f-061) — Session cookie without flags (`frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:104`) (`frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:104`)

**Affected components:** [C-01](#c-01)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — encode output through the framework's contextual escaping and add a strict `Content-Security-Policy` as defence in depth
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-061](#m-061) — Set secure session-cookie attributes

<a id="w-014"></a>
### W-014 — Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

🟡 **Medium** · design weakness · design-risk

Browser rendering paths write HTML or DOM content directly without one enforced contextual-encoding or sanitisation boundary. Safety depends on each individual component using the right API.

**Architecture evidence:** Template Autoescape, Output Encoding, Content Security Policy

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — use framework-safe rendering by default and isolate any required raw HTML behind one reviewed sanitisation and Trusted Types policy

<a id="th-01"></a><a id="th-02"></a><a id="th-03"></a><a id="th-06"></a><a id="th-11"></a><a id="th-04"></a><a id="th-07"></a><a id="th-08"></a><a id="th-09"></a><a id="th-12"></a><a id="th-13"></a><a id="th-14"></a><a id="th-17"></a><a id="th-16"></a><a id="th-18"></a>

### 🔴 Critical (8)

<a id="t-001"></a><a id="f-001"></a>
#### F-001 · JWT Verification Without Algorithm Allowlist

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (6)

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → api-server · Authentication: The boundary assumes `expressJwt` proves caller identity, but the verifier accepts an attacker-chosen algorithm, so the authentication leg does not hold.

**Instances (6):** 🔴 `lib/insecurity.ts:189`, 🟠 `routes/chat.ts:45`, 🔴 `lib/insecurity.ts:52`, 🟠 `lib/insecurity.ts:53`, 🟠 `lib/insecurity.ts:56`, 🔴 `routes/verify.ts:120`

**Issue:** An anonymous internet attacker forges a JWT for any account because neither JWT verification path constrains the accepted algorithm. `updateAuthenticatedUsers` calls `jwt.verify(token, publicKey, cb)` with no `algorithms` option, and `isAuthorized()` builds `expressJwt({ secret: publicKey })` the same way.

The verification key is the `RS256` public key loaded from `encryptionkeys/jwt.pub`, which ships in the repository, so the attacker signs an `HS256` token using that public key text as the HMAC secret and both verifiers accept it. Complete authentication bypass for every REST route on port 3000, including admin-only functions, order `data`, wallets, and payment methods.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/insecurity.ts:189` calls `jwt.verify` with only a key and a callback; no `algorithms` allowlist is passed, and the key at line 20 is an asymmetric public key.

```typescript
// lib/insecurity.ts:189
export const updateAuthenticatedUsers = () => (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  const token = req.cookies.token || utils.jwtFrom(req)
  if (token && authenticatedUsers.get(token) === undefined) {
    jwt.verify(token, publicKey, (err: Error | null, decoded: any) => {
      if (err === null && decoded?.data !== undefined) {
        authenticatedUsers.put(token, decoded)
        res.cookie('token', token)
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown `algorithms` → ● [M-001](#m-001) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (`insecurity.ts:189`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.2](#32-jwt-verification-without-algorithm-allowlist-in-express-js-api-server)

<a id="t-004"></a><a id="f-004"></a>
#### F-004 · Hardcoded RSA signing key in source (lib/insecurity.ts:21)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → api-server · Authentication: The internet-facing boundary relies on JWT issuance to establish identity, but the signing key is a committed literal any reader can reuse to mint tokens.<br>[tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → auth-service · Authentication: A token forged with the in-source private key satisfies the boundary without any `password` verification, breaking its authentication leg.

**Issue:** An attacker who can read the repository mints valid session tokens offline because the RSA private key used to sign every JWT is a string literal. `authorize()` signs all authentication tokens with that constant, and `deluxeToken()` derives the deluxe membership HMAC from the same secret, so one disclosed literal defeats both session issuance and the deluxe entitlement check.

Offline forgery of any `user` or admin session and of deluxe entitlement tokens, with no server-side signal that the token was not issued by the application.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/insecurity.ts:21` assigns a complete PEM-encoded RSA private key to a module constant that `lib/insecurity.ts:54` uses as the JWT signing key.

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ● [M-004](#m-004) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (`insecurity.ts:21`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.5](#35-hardcoded-rsa-signing-key-in-source)

<a id="t-005"></a><a id="f-005"></a>
#### F-005 · Hard-coded seeded account credentials (data/datacreator.ts:196)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - SQLite Database  ·  **Location:** `data/datacreator.ts:196`

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker reads the shipped static `user` `data`, then authenticates as the seeded administrator because `createUsers` writes the configured `password` verbatim into the Users table at every startup, giving full application authority without any credential guess. Full compromise of the persisted `data` set: `user` accounts, orders, basket items, payment cards, addresses, and wallet balances are readable and modifiable by anyone holding the shipped credential.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `createUsers` loads records through `loadStaticUserData` and passes the static `password` field straight into `UserModel.create` at line 196, so the seeded credential is fixed for every deployment of this build.

```typescript
// data/datacreator.ts:196
        const user = await UserModel.create({
          username,
          email: completeEmail,
          password,
          role,
          deluxeToken: role === security.roles.deluxe ? security.deluxeToken(completeEmail) : '',
          profileImage: `assets/public/images/uploads/${profileImage ?? (role === security.roles.admin ? 'defaultAdmin.png' : 'default.svg')}`,
```

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ● [M-005](#m-005) — Move secrets to a managed secret store (`datacreator.ts:196`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.6](#36-hard-coded-seeded-account-credentials-in-datacreator)

<a id="t-006"></a><a id="f-006"></a>
#### F-006 · Predictable derived password (frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Angular SPA  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**Issue:** An attacker who knows any OAuth `user`'s e-mail address computes that `user`'s shop `password` offline as `btoa(email reversed)`, because OAuthComponent registers and logs in the account with exactly that value; the attacker then authenticates through the ordinary `/rest/user/login` form without touching Google at all. Every account created through the Google login path is takeable by anyone who learns the e-mail address, giving access to that `user`'s basket, order history, addresses, and stored payment methods, and to the admin panel if an administrator ever signed in through OAuth.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `oauth.component.ts:30` sets `const password = btoa(profile.email.split('').reverse().join(''))` and passes it to `userService.save()`, and line 46 reuses the identical expression to log in, so the credential is a pure function of the public identity claim.

```typescript
// frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30
  ngOnInit (): void {
    this.userService.oauthLogin(this.parseRedirectUrlParams().access_token).subscribe({
      next: (profile: any) => {
        const password = btoa(profile.email.split('').reverse().join(''))
        this.userService.save({ email: profile.email, password, passwordRepeat: password }).subscribe({
          next: () => {
            this.login(profile)
```

**Fix:** ● [M-006](#m-006) — Use OAuth authorization code flow with PKCE (`oauth.component.ts:30`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-522](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/522.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.7](#37-predictable-derived-password-in-oauth)

<a id="t-007"></a><a id="f-007"></a>
#### F-007 · SQL Injection

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-5](#tb-5) - api-server → sqlite-database · Query construction: The SQLite boundary assumes every query reaches the database through Sequelize parameterization, but the login path emits a fully interpolated raw statement.<br>[tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → auth-service · Authentication: Injected SQL makes the login query return a `user` row without a password-hash match, so a session token is issued without credential verification.

**Instances (2):** `routes/login.ts:34`, `routes/search.ts:23`

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker authenticates as any account because `routes/login.ts:34` builds the login query by interpolating `req.body.email` directly into `SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '...' AND password = '...'` and passes it to `models.sequelize.query`. No parameter binding or escaping is applied, so a quote plus comment sequence in the email field truncates the `password` predicate and the first returned row is handed to `afterLogin`, which issues a full session token.

Authentication bypass into arbitrary accounts including administrators, plus arbitrary read of the Users table through the same injection point.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/login.ts:34` concatenates `req.body.email` into a raw SQL string passed to `sequelize.query`, with no bind parameters.

```typescript
// routes/login.ts:34

  return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    verifyPreLoginChallenges(req) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
    models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '${req.body.email || ''}' AND password = '${security.hash(req.body.password || '')}' AND deletedAt IS NULL`, { model: UserModel, plain: true }) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
      .then((authenticatedUser) => { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
        const user = utils.queryResultToJson(authenticatedUser)
        if (user.data?.id && user.data.totpSecret !== '') {
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-007](#m-007) — Use parameterized database queries (`login.ts:34`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.3](#33-sql-injection-in-login)

<a id="t-008"></a><a id="f-008"></a>
#### F-008 · Insecure Direct Object Reference

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (20)

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - `Authorization` is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → api-server · `Authorization`: The authorization leg assumes object access is checked against the authenticated identity, but the credited amount itself is taken from the request with no capture proof.

**Instances (20):** 🟠 `routes/wallet.ts:27`, 🔴 `routes/address.ts:11`, 🔴 `routes/address.ts:18`, 🔴 `routes/address.ts:29`, 🟠 `routes/basketItems.ts:68`, 🔴 `routes/dataExport.ts:26`, 🟠 `routes/delivery.ts:34`, 🔴 `routes/deluxe.ts:25` … (+12 more)

**Issue:** Server-side authorization MUST derive the resource owner from the authenticated session (`req.user` / `req.session` / `req.auth`), never from attacker-controlled request `data`. Trusting `req.body.UserId` etc. enables horizontal privilege escalation across all authenticated tenants.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
// routes/wallet.ts:27
    const card = cardId ? await CardModel.findOne({ where: { id: cardId, UserId: req.body.UserId } }) : null
    if (card != null) {
      try {
        await WalletModel.increment({ balance: req.body.balance }, { where: { UserId: req.body.UserId } })
        res.status(200).json({ status: 'success', data: req.body.balance })
      } catch {
        res.status(404).json({ status: 'error' })
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting `user`'s identity and reject cross-tenant references → ● [M-008](#m-008) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (`wallet.ts:27`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-009"></a><a id="f-009"></a>
#### F-009 · Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Angular SPA  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (6)

**Instances (6):** 🔴 `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143`, 🟠 `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`, 🟠 `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73`, 🟠 `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`, 🟠 `frontend/src/app/data-export/data-export.component.ts:71`, 🟡 `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim a link such as /#`/search?q`=`<img src=x onerror=...>`, and `filterTable()` passes the raw query parameter through `DomSanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml` into the `searchValue` binding, which the template renders with [`innerHTML`]; the payload executes in the shop origin with no `Content-Security-Policy` to stop it and reads the JWT the SPA keeps in `localStorage`. Any `user` who opens an attacker-crafted search link loses their session JWT and can have arbitrary requests issued as themselves; if the victim is an administrator the same payload reaches the admin panel and its `user` and feedback management actions.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `search-result.component.ts:136-143` reads `route.snapshot.queryParams.q` and assigns `this.searchValue` = `sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam)`; `search-result.component.html:11` binds that value with [`innerHTML`], completing an unescaped source-to-sink path from the URL.

```typescript
// frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143
        this.io.socket().emit('verifyLocalXssChallenge', queryParam)
      }) // vuln-code-snippet hide-end
      this.dataSource.filter = queryParam.toLowerCase()
      this.searchValue = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line localXssChallenge xssBonusChallenge
      if (this.gridDataSourceSubscription) {
        this.gridDataSourceSubscription.unsubscribe()
      }
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ● [M-009](#m-009) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`search-result.component.ts:143`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.4](#34-cross-site-scripting-xss-in-search-result)

<a id="t-010"></a><a id="f-010"></a>
#### F-010 · Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body routes/verify.ts:53

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Instances (2):** 🟠 `models/user.ts:83`, 🔴 `routes/verify.ts:53`

**Issue:** Server code that consumes `req.body.role` / `req.body.isAdmin` / etc. without an explicit allowlist trusts the client to behave. An attacker simply adds `{"role":"admin"}` to their request to escalate.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
// routes/verify.ts:53

export const registerAdminChallenge = () => (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.registerAdminChallenge, () => {
    return req.body && req.body.role === security.roles.admin
  })
  next()
}
```

**Fix:** ● [M-010](#m-010) — Allowlist client-controlled fields (`verify.ts:53`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.1](#31-mass-assignment-privileged-field-accepted-from-request-body)

### 🟠 High (31)

<a id="t-002"></a><a id="f-002"></a>
#### F-002 · Missing Authentication on State-Changing Routes

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (19)

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - `Authorization` is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → api-server · Authentication: The authentication leg assumes every state-changing route is registered behind `expressJwt`, but three upload routes are registered before that middleware is installed.

**Instances (19):** 🟠 `server.ts:309`, 🟡 `routes/nftMint.ts:44`, 🟠 `server.ts:310`, 🟠 `server.ts:311`, 🟠 `server.ts:408`, 🟠 `server.ts:420`, 🟠 `server.ts:421`, 🟠 `server.ts:422` … (+11 more)

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker reaches the archive and markup parsers because `server.ts:309` registers `POST /file-upload` with only `multer` and the parsing chain `handleZipFileUpload, handleXmlUpload, handleYamlUpload`, and the authorization block that installs `security.updateAuthenticatedUsers()` and the route guards does not begin until `server.ts:354`. The image endpoints and `server.ts:311` are registered in the same unguarded window; only `/rest/memories` carries `security.appendUserId()`.

Registration order therefore places three attacker-reachable parsers outside the authentication boundary entirely. Anonymous callers feed arbitrary archives and markup into server-side parsers and write into the publicly served uploads directory, without ever presenting a credential.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:309` registers the file-upload route and its zip, XML, and YAML handlers with no security middleware, 45 lines before the authorization block starts `at ``server.ts:354`.

```typescript
// server.ts:309
  app.use(bodyParser.urlencoded({ extended: true }))
  /* File Upload */
  app.post('/file-upload', uploadToMemory.single('file'), ensureFileIsPassed, metrics.observeFileUploadMetricsMiddleware(), checkUploadSize, checkFileType, handleZipFileUpload, handleXmlUpload, handleYamlUpload)
  app.post('/profile/image/file', uploadToMemory.single('file'), ensureFileIsPassed, metrics.observeFileUploadMetricsMiddleware(), utils.asyncHandler(profileImageFileUpload()))
  app.post('/profile/image/url', uploadToMemory.single('file'), utils.asyncHandler(profileImageUrlUpload()))
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-002](#m-002) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (`server.ts:309`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-011"></a><a id="f-011"></a>

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Authentication and Session Service  ·  **Location:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:10`

**Issue:** An attacker who can research or guess the answer can satisfy the recovery check and take over the account without the original credential.

```typescript
import { SecurityAnswerModel } from '../models/securityAnswer'
import * as challengeUtils from '../lib/challengeUtils'

```

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-012"></a><a id="f-012"></a>
#### F-012 · Unsigned container publish (.github/workflows/ci.yml:327)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:327`

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** An attacker who obtains the `DOCKERHUB_TOKEN` repository secret authenticates and pushes a backdoored image over the bkimminich/`juice-shop:latest` tag written on line 345. No step in any workflow signs the image or emits a provenance attestation, so a downstream `docker pull` cannot distinguish the attacker's image from a genuine pipeline build.

Consumers pulling bkimminich/`juice-shop:latest` execute attacker-supplied code under the project's own name, with no cryptographic means to detect the substitution and no way to bind a published image back to a specific pipeline run.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `ci.yml:327` authenticates the Docker Hub push with the long-lived `DOCKERHUB_TOKEN` repository secret rather than a workflow-scoped OIDC credential, and no workflow under .github/workflows requests id-token, invokes cosign, or produces a provenance attestation.

```yaml
// .github/workflows/ci.yml:327
        with:
          username: ${{ secrets.DOCKERHUB_USERNAME }}
          password: ${{ secrets.DOCKERHUB_TOKEN }}
      - name: "Set tag & labels for ${{ github.ref }}"
        run: |
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-012](#m-012) — Verify token signatures before trusting claims (`ci.yml:327`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-013"></a><a id="f-013"></a>
#### F-013 · Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → api-server · Authentication: The Socket\.IO server is attached to the same Express HTTP server the boundary protects, but the `handshake` bypasses the `security.isAuthorized()` `expressJwt` leg entirely.

**Instances (3):** 🟠 `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`, 🟠 `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:36`, 🟡 `frontend/src/app/Services/socket-io.service.ts:22`

**Issue:** An attacker points a non-browser Socket\.IO client at port 3000 and completes the `handshake` without presenting any credential. The server constructs the Socket\.IO server with a single `cors.origin` option and registers no `handshake` authentication, and CORS is a browser-enforced response header rather than a server-side admission check, so a scripted client bypasses it entirely and is accepted onto the push channel as an anonymous peer indistinguishable from the legitimate Angular frontend.

Any internet client reaches the live event channel of the same process that serves the JWT-protected REST API on port 3000, gaining read access to challenge `notifications` and write access to the event handlers that mutate challenge and notification state.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20` constructs the Socket\.IO server with `cors: { origin: 'http://localhost:4200' }` as its only connection-level option, and the connection handler at line 23 runs for every socket without inspecting a credential. A grep of both component files for `io.use(`, `allowRequest`, `handshake`, `verifyToken`, `isAuthorized`, and `authenticate` returned zero hits, so no `handshake` credential is verified anywhere on this channel.

```typescript
// lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20

const registerWebsocketEvents = (server: any) => {
  const io = new Server(server, { cors: { origin: 'http://localhost:4200' } })
  globalWithSocketIO.io = io

```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-013](#m-013) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`)

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-014"></a><a id="f-014"></a>
#### F-014 · Plaintext TOTP secret column (models/user.ts:112)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - SQLite Database  ·  **Location:** `models/user.ts:112`

**Issue:** An attacker who obtains read access to the database file or to any query result exposing the Users table reads the `totpSecret` column verbatim and generates valid second-factor codes for the affected account, defeating two-factor authentication without touching the victim's device. Two-factor authentication no longer adds a barrier once the database file leaks: an attacker with a cracked or reset `password` mints valid codes and reaches accounts holding orders, payment cards, and wallet balances.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The User model declares `totpSecret` as a plain `DataTypes.STRING` with no setter, `hash`, or encryption wrapper, unlike the `password` and security-answer columns which pass through a transform, so the shared secret is persisted exactly as issued.

```typescript
// models/user.ts:112
        defaultValue: '/assets/public/images/uploads/default.svg'
      },
      totpSecret: {
        type: DataTypes.STRING,
        defaultValue: ''
```

**Fix:** Encrypt the `data` in transit and at rest with vetted primitives → ◕ [M-014](#m-014) — Encrypt the totpSecret column (`user.ts:112`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-311](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/311.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-015"></a><a id="f-015"></a>
#### F-015 · Implicit OAuth flow (frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Angular SPA  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** An attacker lures a victim to a page that redirects to the shop's own OAuth entry point, or replays a fragment of their choosing, because `googleLogin()` starts a bare implicit flow with `response_type=token` and sends no state, nonce, or PKCE challenge; the route matcher then accepts any URL carrying '#`access_token`=' (`frontend/src/app/app.routing.ts:286`) and OAuthComponent parses the fragment by string splitting and posts the token straight to `/rest/user/oauth` without checking that this browser started the flow. A victim can be silently logged into an account the attacker controls (session fixation on the shop), or an attacker-obtained Google access token can be injected into the victim's browser to bind their shop session to a foreign identity; orders, addresses, and payment `data` are then created under the wrong account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:148` builds the authorization URL with `response_type=token` and only `client_id`, scope, and `redirect_uri`; no state or nonce parameter is generated or stored, and `oauth.component.ts:70-79` parses the returned fragment without comparing any request-binding value.

```typescript
// frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148

  googleLogin () {
    this.windowRefService.nativeWindow.location.replace(`${oauthProviderUrl}?client_id=${this.clientId}&response_type=token&scope=email&redirect_uri=${this.redirectUri}`)
  }
}
```

**Fix:** Strengthen authentication: enforce a vetted JWT verifier with explicit algorithm, MFA where appropriate → ◕ [M-015](#m-015) — Harden the authentication flow (`login.component.ts:148`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-016"></a><a id="f-016"></a>
#### F-016 · Prompt injection via unvalidated messages (routes/chat.ts:191)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:191`

**Issue:** LLM01 - Prompt Injection: an anonymous caller overrides the chatbot's behavioural rules because `routes/chat.ts:191` reads the entire `req.body.messages` array with no schema, `role`, or length validation and passes it verbatim to `streamText` alongside the trusted `system` prompt. Nothing filters attacker-supplied entries with `role: 'system'` or `role: 'assistant'`, so injected turns are indistinguishable from the server's own instructions.

The coupon policy `at ``routes/chat.ts:98-103` is prompt text only, with no check outside the model, so an injected instruction reaches the `generateCoupon` tool. An attacker dictates chatbot behaviour, extracts the `system` prompt, and drives the coupon-issuing tool outside the policy the prompt describes.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/chat.ts:191` assigns `req.body.messages` to a local with only a nullish-coalescing default, and no validation runs before the array reaches `streamText` at line 206.

```typescript
// routes/chat.ts:191

    const model = config.get<string>('application.chatBot.model')
    const messages = req.body?.messages ?? []
    const userName = await getUserNameFromToken(req)

```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-016](#m-016) — Validate and constrain the chat messages array (`chat.ts:191`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-74](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/74.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/dataErasure.ts:104`

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

**Issue:** A request-controlled path with `../` can read arbitrary files (`/etc/passwd`, source, secrets) or write outside the intended root.

```typescript
// routes/dataErasure.ts:104

      if (req.body.layout) {
        const filePath: string = path.resolve(req.body.layout).toLowerCase()
        const isForbiddenFile: boolean = (filePath.includes('ftp') || filePath.includes('ctf.key') || filePath.includes('encryptionkeys'))
```
