**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-018"></a><a id="f-018"></a>
#### F-018 · Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token lib/insecurity.ts:53

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Authentication and Session Service  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:53`

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Security-sensitive `data` uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** A predictable token/secret lets an attacker guess or brute-force session identifiers, reset links, or OTPs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
// lib/insecurity.ts:53

export const isAuthorized = () => expressJwt(({ secret: publicKey }) as any)
export const denyAll = () => expressJwt({ secret: '' + Math.random() } as any)
export const authorize = (user = {}) => jwt.sign(user, privateKey, { expiresIn: '6h', algorithm: 'RS256' })
export const verify = (token: string) => token ? (jws.verify as ((token: string, secret: string) => boolean))(token, publicKey) : false
```

**Fix:** Switch to a cryptographically secure RNG (`crypto.randomBytes` / OS `/dev/urandom`) → ◕ [M-018](#m-018) — Use cryptographically secure random values (`insecurity.ts:53`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-330](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/330.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-019"></a><a id="f-019"></a>
#### F-019 · Use of Unmaintained Third-Party Components

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33`

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Vulnerable & Outdated Dependencies is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** Whoever controls the main branch of calibreapp/image-actions pushes a commit that GitHub resolves the next time `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33` runs, executing that code inside this repository's runner with the `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN` handed to it on line 35. A branch reference is re-resolved on every run, so no review of the repository can pin what actually executes.

Arbitrary third-party code executes in the repository's CI with a repository-scoped `GITHUB_TOKEN`, allowing modification of source and workflow files that subsequently flow into the published Docker image and the packaged release archive.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `image_actions.yml:33` references `calibreapp/image-actions@main`, a mutable branch ref that GitHub re-resolves on every run, and line 35 passes `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN` to it. The same file also references actions/checkout@v6 (line 30) and `peter-evans/create-pull-request@v8` (line 42) by tag, and `ci.yml:188` references `coverallsapp/github-action@v2` by tag.

```yaml
// .github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33
      - name: Compress Images
        id: calibre
        uses: calibreapp/image-actions@main
        with:
          githubToken: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
```

**Fix:** Replace the unmaintained dependency with a maintained equivalent or fork it under ownership → ◕ [M-019](#m-019) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest (`image_actions.yml:33`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-020"></a><a id="f-020"></a>
#### F-020 · Release built without dependency lockfile (.github/workflows/release.yml:55)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:55`

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Issue:** An attacker who publishes a malicious version inside any transitive dependency's `semver` range gets it installed at release time, because `.github/workflows/release.yml:55` runs `npm install --production` with no lockfile committed to the repository and without `--ignore-scripts`. The package's install hook executes on the runner and its code is packaged by `npm run package:ci` on line 56 into the archive that softprops/action-gh-release attaches to the release on line 58.

Every published release archive and Docker image can carry code that was never reviewed in a pull request, and the absence of a lockfile means no `diff` signal exists to detect the substitution after the fact.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `release.yml:55` installs with `npm install --production`, which resolves versions against the current registry state and executes lifecycle scripts. No `package-lock.json` exists in the repository and no workflow under .github/workflows uses `npm ci`, so no build in the pipeline is reproducible from a committed dependency baseline.

```yaml
// .github/workflows/release.yml:55
      - name: "Package application"
        run: |
          npm install --production
          npm run package:ci
      - name: 'Attach packaged archive to tag release'
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-020](#m-020) — Set least-privilege CI workflow permissions (`release.yml:55`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1357](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1357.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-021"></a><a id="f-021"></a>
#### F-021 · Client-trusted security decision (lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41`

**Issue:** An attacker emits a `verifyLocalXssChallenge` event whose payload is simply the literal string the server searches for. The handler passes that client-supplied value directly into `challengeUtils.solveIf`, which treats a substring match on attacker-controlled `data` as proof that the XSS payload actually rendered in a victim browser, and then persists `solved = true`.

The same pattern applies to `verifySvgInjectionChallenge` and `verifyCloseNotificationsChallenge`, so an attacker forges completion of security exercises without triggering the underlying vulnerability at all. Challenge and scoreboard state, the integrity signal this application exists to produce, can be written by any anonymous client, so recorded training progress and CTF scoring no longer reflect actual exploitation.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41` passes the raw event payload `data` into `utils.contains(data, '<iframe src="javascript:alert">')` as the completion criterion, and line 50 accepts any client array of length greater than one. The criterion is evaluated over `data` the client fully controls, and `lib/challengeUtils.ts:31` shows the resulting `solve()` call persists the state with `await challenge.save()`.

```typescript
// lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41

    socket.on('verifyLocalXssChallenge', (data: any) => {
      challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.localXssChallenge, () => { return utils.contains(data, '<iframe src="javascript:alert(`xss`)">') })
      challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.xssBonusChallenge, () => { return utils.contains(data, config.get('challenges.xssBonusPayload')) })
    })
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-021](#m-021) — Use trusted client IPs for rate limiting (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41`)

**Classification:** Insecure Real-Time Channel · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-807](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/807.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-022"></a><a id="f-022"></a>
#### F-022 · Legacy sanitizer on persisted username (models/user.ts:49)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - SQLite Database  ·  **Location:** `models/user.ts:49`

**Issue:** An attacker submits markup in the username field and the User model persists it after passing it through `sanitizeLegacy` instead of `sanitizeSecure`, so the stored row keeps payload that the weaker filter does not remove and that every later consumer of the username reads back as markup. Stored markup in user-controlled columns is served back to other users and administrators, which can hijack their sessions and, through an administrator session, expose the order, address, and payment card rows held in this database.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The username setter branches on a runtime challenge `flag` and calls `security.sanitizeLegacy` `at ``models/user.ts:49`, taking a deliberately weaker path than the `sanitizeSecure` call on line 51; the same pattern selects the weaker `sanitizeHtml` `at ``models/feedback.ts:44`.

```typescript
// models/user.ts:49
        set (username: string) {
          if (utils.isChallengeEnabled(challenges.persistedXssUserChallenge)) {
            username = security.sanitizeLegacy(username)
          } else {
            username = security.sanitizeSecure(username)
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-022](#m-022) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`user.ts:49`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-023"></a><a id="f-023"></a>
#### F-023 · SSRF via profile image URL (routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`

**Issue:** A low-privilege authenticated `user` makes the server issue arbitrary outbound requests because `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24` calls `fetch(url)` with `req.body.imageUrl` taken verbatim from `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:19`. No scheme or host allowlist is applied, so the attacker targets loopback services, RFC 1918 addresses, or a cloud metadata endpoint.

When the fetch fails, the catch branch stores the raw URL on the `user` profile, turning response behaviour and timing into an oracle for what the server could reach. Internal network and cloud metadata reachable from the API server can be probed, and retrieved content is written into the publicly served uploads directory.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24` passes the unvalidated `req.body.imageUrl` string directly to fetch, with no destination allowlist between the assignment at line 19 and the call.

```typescript
// routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24
      if (loggedInUser) {
        try {
          const response = await fetch(url)
          if (!response.ok || !response.body) {
            throw new Error('url returned a non-OK status code or an empty body')
```

**Fix:** Validate the URL scheme + host against an explicit allow-list before issuing outbound requests → ◕ [M-023](#m-023) — Validate and allowlist outbound request targets (`profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`)

**Classification:** Server-Side Request Forgery · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-024"></a><a id="f-024"></a>
#### F-024 · Unbounded field selection leaks credentials (routes/currentUser.ts:31)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Authentication and Session Service  ·  **Location:** `routes/currentUser.ts:31`

**Issue:** An attacker hosts a page that loads GET `/rest/user/whoami?fields=password`,`totpSecret`&`callback=steal` as a script; the victim browser attaches the token cookie the endpoint reads, the loop at line 31 copies whichever `user` attributes were named into the response, and line 58 returns them as executable JSONP to the attacker origin. The victim's `MD5` `password` `hash` and TOTP shared secret leave the application to an attacker-controlled origin, giving offline `password` recovery and the ability to generate valid second-factor codes for that account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/currentUser.ts:31` assigns `baseUser[field]` from `user.data` for every comma-separated name in the fields query parameter with no allowlist, and line 58 serves that object via `res.jsonp` when a callback parameter is present.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◕ [M-024](#m-024) — Stop exposing internal information to clients (`currentUser.ts:31`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-025"></a><a id="f-025"></a>
#### F-025 · MD5 password hashing (lib/insecurity.ts:41)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Authentication and Session Service  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:41`

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Security-sensitive `data` uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** An attacker who obtains the Users table through the login SQL injection or the whoami field selector runs the recovered digests through a commodity `MD5` cracking rig, recovering the plaintext passwords of every account because `lib/insecurity.ts:41` hashes credentials with a single unsalted `MD5` pass. Recovered plaintext passwords expose every `user` account and enable credential reuse against unrelated services, converting a single database read into a permanent, application-wide credential compromise.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/insecurity.ts:41` defines the `password` `hash` as `crypto.createHash('md5').update(data).digest('hex')` with no salt and no work factor, and `routes/login.ts:34`, `routes/2fa.ts:107` and `routes/changePassword.ts:39` all compare against that digest.

```typescript
// lib/insecurity.ts:41
}

export const hash = (data: string) => crypto.createHash('md5').update(data).digest('hex')
export const hmac = (data: string) => crypto.createHmac('sha256', 'pa4qacea4VK9t9nGv7yZtwmj').update(data).digest('hex')

```

**Fix:** Replace the broken `hash` with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◕ [M-025](#m-025) — Use modern cryptographic hash and KDF algorithms (`insecurity.ts:41`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-328](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/328.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-026"></a><a id="f-026"></a>
#### F-026 · Bot PAT exposed to later steps (.github/workflows/update-challenges-www.yml:19)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/update-challenges-www.yml:19`

**Issue:** The checkout authenticates with `secrets.BOT_TOKEN`, a token carrying write access to the separate repository juice-shop/juice-shop.github.io, and does not set `persist-credentials: false`, so actions/checkout writes that token into the workspace .git/config as an HTTP extraheader. The two shell steps on lines 27 and 36 interpolate \${{ `github.ref_name` }} directly into their command lines, and the third-party stefanzweifel/git-auto-commit-action runs in the same job on line 45, giving several routes for injected or compromised code to read the token and use it against a repository outside this one's permission model.

A token with write access to three repositories in two GitHub organisations leaves the CI trust boundary, letting the holder publish content on the project's public website and companion guide under the project's own bot identity.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `update-challenges-www.yml:19` passes `secrets.BOT_TOKEN` to actions/checkout for the external repository juice-shop/juice-shop.github.io. No workflow under .github/workflows sets `persist-credentials: false`, and lines 28 and 37 interpolate \${{ `github.ref_name` }} into a `run:` shell block. The same `BOT_TOKEN` pattern repeats in `update-challenges-www-legacy.yml:19`, `update-challenges-ebook.yml:18`, `update-news-www.yml:14` and `update-news-www-legacy.yml:14`.

```yaml
// .github/workflows/update-challenges-www.yml:19
      uses: actions/checkout@11bd71901bbe5b1630ceea73d27597364c9af683 #v4.2.2
      with:
        token: ${{ secrets.BOT_TOKEN }}
        repository: juice-shop/juice-shop.github.io
        ref: master
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-026](#m-026) — Scope BOT_TOKEN to a GitHub App installation token and set persist-credentials… (`update-challenges-www.yml:19`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-522](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/522.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-027"></a><a id="f-027"></a>
#### F-027 · Secret exposure to anonymous client (lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:30)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:30`

**Issue:** An attacker opens an unauthenticated socket and the connection handler immediately replays the entire queued `notifications` array to it. Each notification carries the challenge CTF `flag` generated by `utils.ctfFlag`, and the `hidden` field that is meant to suppress display when `challenges.showSolvedNotifications` is off is only a rendering hint the client is trusted to honour, so `notifications` the operator configured as `hidden` are still transmitted in full to any peer that connects.

CTF flags and solved-challenge details, the credential material this application issues for scoring, are readable by any anonymous internet client, and the configured suppression of solved-challenge `notifications` is defeated because suppression happens only in the browser.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:30` emits every entry of the shared `notifications` array to each newly connected socket without any authorization check. `lib/challengeUtils.ts:52` shows each notification is built with `const flag = utils.ctfFlag(challenge.name)`, line 67 sets `hidden` from configuration while still including the `flag` in the payload, and line 75 broadcasts the same object to all connected sockets.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◕ [M-027](#m-027) — Stop exposing internal information to clients (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:30`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-028"></a><a id="f-028"></a>
#### F-028 · Plaintext payment card number storage (models/card.ts:38)

**Severity:** 🟠 High _(raw Critical)_  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - SQLite Database  ·  **Location:** `models/card.ts:38`

**Issue:** An attacker who reads the SQLite file, a backup copy, or any query result over the Cards table recovers complete sixteen-digit card numbers together with the cardholder name and expiry, because the model persists the full number as a plain integer with no encryption, tokenization, or truncation. Full payment card records including holder name and expiry are exposed in one read of an unencrypted file that sits on the same filesystem as the application process, which is a direct cardholder-data breach.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `models/card.ts:38-45` declares `cardNum` as `DataTypes.INTEGER` constrained only to the sixteen-digit range, with `fullName`, `expMonth`, and `expYear` stored alongside it, and no setter transforms the value; a search for encryption in models/ and `data/datacreator.ts` returned no hit.

```typescript
// models/card.ts:38
      },
      fullName: DataTypes.STRING,
      cardNum: {
        type: DataTypes.INTEGER,
        validate: {
```

**Fix:** Encrypt the `data` in transit and at rest with vetted primitives → ◕ [M-028](#m-028) — Tokenize or encrypt the cardNum column (`card.ts:38`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-311](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/311.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-029"></a><a id="f-029"></a>
#### F-029 · Unsalted fast password hash (models/user.ts:76)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - SQLite Database  ·  **Location:** `models/user.ts:76`

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Security-sensitive `data` uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** An attacker who obtains the database file or any query result exposing the `password` column recovers the cleartext passwords offline, because the User model stores a single-call digest of the `password` with no per-user salt and no work factor, so precomputed tables apply directly. Every account `password` in the store, including the administrator and accounting roles, is recoverable offline, which yields access to orders, addresses, payment cards, and wallet balances held in the same database.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The `password` setter `at ``models/user.ts:76` stores `security.hash(clearTextPassword)` as the whole column value, and the model declares no salt or iteration attribute anywhere in its attribute list, so identical passwords produce identical stored values.

**Fix:** Replace the broken `hash` with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◕ [M-029](#m-029) — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm (`user.ts:76`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-030"></a><a id="f-030"></a>
#### F-030 · Session token in localStorage (frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Angular SPA  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`

**Issue:** An attacker who achieves script execution through any of this component's HTML-injection sinks, or who has possession of the device, reads the session JWT directly with `localStorage.getItem('token')`, because the SPA persists the token in `localStorage` on every login path and re-reads it from there for routing and API calls. One successful injection or one moment of device access hands over a full session token that authenticates every subsequent API call, including administrative ones when the victim holds the admin `role`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:101` stores the authentication token in `localStorage`; the same write occurs `at ``oauth.component.ts:51` and `two-factor-auth-enter.component.ts:53`, and `request.interceptor.ts:13` and `app.guard.ts:18` read it back, so no HttpOnly boundary ever protects it.

**Fix:** ◕ [M-030](#m-030) — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies (`login.component.ts:101`)

**Classification:** Insecure Client-Side Storage · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-031"></a><a id="f-031"></a>
#### F-031 · Hardcoded wallet mnemonic in source (routes/checkKeys.ts:10)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker reads the twelve-word BIP-39 mnemonic committed, replays `HDNodeWallet.fromPhrase` locally to derive the same private key the server derives at line 11, and thereby holds the application's blockchain identity without ever touching the running service. The application's blockchain identity is fully compromised: an attacker can drain the wallet's balance, transfer or mint NFTs under that identity, and the mnemonic cannot be rotated without a code change and redeployment.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/checkKeys.ts:10` stores the mnemonic as a source literal and line 11 derives the wallet from it, so the private key at line 12 is reproducible by anyone with read access to the repository.

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ◕ [M-031](#m-031) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (`checkKeys.ts:10`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-032"></a><a id="f-032"></a>
#### F-032 · Login endpoint without rate limiting (server.ts:596)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → auth-service · `Authorization`: The boundary assumes credential endpoints `throttle` by IP and account, but the login handler applies no limiter at all on either dimension.

**Instances (2):** `server.ts:596`, `routes/login.ts:32`

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker runs unbounded credential stuffing against `POST /rest/user/login` because `server.ts:596` registers the route with only `login()` and no throttling middleware. Rate limiting is applied in this file for `password` reset and, 466, and 472 for the 2FA routes, and nowhere else, so the primary authentication endpoint is the one credential-guessing surface with no bound.

Each attempt also runs the raw SQL query `at ``routes/login.ts:34`, so the load falls straight on SQLite. Unlimited `password` guessing against every account and sustained database load from an anonymous source, with no lockout and no logged evidence.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:596` registers the login route with no rate-limiting middleware, while `rateLimit` is demonstrably applied on four other routes in the same file.

```typescript
// server.ts:596

  /* Custom Restful API */
  app.post('/rest/user/login', login())
  app.get('/rest/user/change-password', utils.asyncHandler(changePassword()))
  app.post('/rest/user/reset-password', utils.asyncHandler(resetPassword()))
```

**Fix:** Apply rate limiting and lock-out thresholds on authentication endpoints → ◕ [M-032](#m-032) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (`server.ts:596`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-033"></a><a id="f-033"></a>
#### F-033 · Unbounded LLM token consumption (server.ts:638)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:638`

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Denial of Service is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** LLM10 - Unbounded Consumption: an anonymous attacker drives arbitrary spend at the external LLM provider because `server.ts:638` registers `POST /rest/chat` with neither authentication nor rate limiting. `routes/chat.ts:206` forwards the caller-supplied message array with no size cap and no output-token limit, while `stopWhen: stepCountIs(10)` `at ``routes/chat.ts:209` permits ten model round-trips per request and `maxRetries` `at ``routes/chat.ts:208` multiplies each of them.

Concurrent requests carrying maximal message payloads therefore consume provider quota and server sockets without bound. Provider quota exhaustion and cost escalation from anonymous traffic, plus long-lived SSE connections that starve the single Express process serving all REST, WebSocket, and static traffic.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:638` registers the chat route with no auth or `throttle` middleware, and `routes/chat.ts:206` passes the unbounded caller message array to `streamText` with no `maxOutputTokens` setting.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◕ [M-033](#m-033) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`server.ts:638`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-034"></a><a id="f-034"></a>
#### F-034 · Rate limiter bypassable via forwarded header (server.ts:346)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:346`

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Vulnerable & Outdated Dependencies is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** An attacker defeats the only password-reset `throttle` because the `keyGenerator` buckets requests by `headers['X-Forwarded-For'] ?? ip`, a value the client fully controls.

With `app.enable('trust proxy')` set and no trusted-proxy allowlist, rotating the header on each request places every attempt in a fresh bucket, so the 100-request window configured `at ``server.ts:344-345` never triggers. Unlimited brute forcing of security answers leading to `password` reset and account takeover, with the intended `throttle` providing no protection.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:346` derives the `rate-limit` key from the client-supplied X-Forwarded-For header rather than from a proxy-validated address.

**Fix:** Add the missing protection mechanism for this surface (CSP / CSRF token / headers) → ◕ [M-034](#m-034) — Key the reset-password limiter on the proxy-validated address (`server.ts:346`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-693](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/693.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-035"></a><a id="f-035"></a>
#### F-035 · Coupon discount unbounded server-side (routes/chat.ts:184)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - `Authorization` is implemented route by route

**Issue:** LLM06 - Excessive Agency: `routes/chat.ts:184` issues a real, redeemable coupon from a discount value the model chose, with no server-side bound and no authorization of the caller. The tool's input schema declares `z.number()` with the ten percent cap stated only in the description text and in the prompt policy `at ``routes/chat.ts:98-103`, so nothing outside the prompt constrains the value.

Combined with the unauthenticated route `at ``server.ts:638` and the unvalidated message array, an anonymous caller injects an instruction that makes the model call the tool with an arbitrary discount. An anonymous attacker mints coupons at any discount percentage, redeemable at checkout, converting a prompt-level policy into unbounded revenue loss.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/chat.ts:184` calls `security.generateCoupon` with the model-supplied discount; the schema at line 179 imposes no maximum and no code path validates the value before issuance.

```typescript
// routes/chat.ts:184
          challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge, () => discount >= 10) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
          challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.chatbotGreedyInjectionChallenge, () => discount >= 50) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
          const couponCode = security.generateCoupon(discount) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge
          return { couponCode, discount } // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge
        }
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-035](#m-035) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (`chat.ts:184`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.8](#38-coupon-discount-unbounded-server-side-in-chat)

<a id="t-036"></a><a id="f-036"></a>
#### F-036 · Deluxe role granted without payment (routes/deluxe.ts:43)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - `Authorization` is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → api-server · `Authorization`: The authorization leg assumes entitlement changes are checked server-side, but the `role` update runs on a path where no payment condition was evaluated.

**Issue:** A customer promotes themselves to the deluxe `role` for free because `routes/deluxe.ts:43` updates the `user`'s `role` and issues a matching `deluxeToken` unconditionally after the two payment branches. Those branches are entered only when `req.body.paymentMode` equals `'wallet'` or `'card'`, and neither branch is mandatory; any other value skips both and falls straight through to the `role` update.

`routes/deluxe.ts:48` then mints a fresh token carrying the elevated `role`, so the escalation persists across requests. Any authenticated customer obtains the paid deluxe entitlement and its associated pricing and content without a charge, a wallet debit, or a valid card.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/deluxe.ts:43` performs the `role` update outside any else-branch or payment assertion, reachable whenever `paymentMode` is neither `'wallet'` nor `'card'`.

```typescript
// routes/deluxe.ts:43

      try {
        const updatedUser = await user.update({ role: security.roles.deluxe, deluxeToken: security.deluxeToken(user.email) })
        challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.freeDeluxeChallenge, () => {
          return security.verify(utils.jwtFrom(req)) && req.body.paymentMode !== 'wallet' && req.body.paymentMode !== 'card'
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-036](#m-036) — Enforce correct server-side authorization (`deluxe.ts:43`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-037"></a><a id="f-037"></a>
#### F-037 · Unverified password change (routes/changePassword.ts:39)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Authentication and Session Service  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**Issue:** An attacker holding any valid token for a victim, whether forged with the in-source signing key or captured through the JSONP whoami leak, calls the change-password endpoint without a current parameter; the guard only runs when `currentPassword` is truthy, so the update at line 51 proceeds and the attacker sets a `password` of their choosing. Any leaked or forged session token converts into permanent ownership of the victim account, including administrative accounts, because the legitimate owner is locked out and the attacker retains a `password` they chose.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/changePassword.ts:39` wraps the current-password comparison in an if (`currentPassword` && ...) condition, so omitting the current query parameter skips verification entirely before `user.update` at line 51 writes the new `password`.

```typescript

    if (currentPassword && security.hash(currentPassword) !== loggedInUser.data.password) {
      res.status(401).send(res.__('Current password is not correct.'))
      return
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-037](#m-037) — Require and verify the current password (`changePassword.ts:39`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-620](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → auth-service · Authentication: An unauthenticated caller crossing this boundary changes an account `password` using only a knowledge answer, with no credential verification and no `throttle`.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker posts a victim email with candidate security answers to `/rest/user/reset-password`; `routes/resetPassword.ts:41` compares only an HMAC of the submitted answer against the stored value, with no proof of mailbox ownership, no second factor and no attempt limit, so a researched or guessed answer sets a new `password` at line 44. Any account whose security answer is guessable or discoverable from public sources, including administrative accounts, is taken over by an unauthenticated internet caller without touching the victim's `password` or second factor.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/resetPassword.ts:41` gates the `password` update solely on `security.hmac(answer)` matching the stored answer for the account identified by the submitted email, and the request never proves control of that mailbox.

