```typescript
        }]
      })
      if ((data != null) && security.hmac(answer) === data.answer) {
        const user = await UserModel.findByPk(data.UserId)
        if (user) {
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-038](#m-038) — Replace security-answer recovery with an emailed single-use reset token (`resetPassword.ts:41`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-039"></a><a id="f-039"></a>
#### F-039 · Comment command without author authorization (.github/workflows/rebase.yml:10)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/rebase.yml:10`

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - `Authorization` is implemented route by route

**Issue:** Any GitHub `user` who can comment on a pull request triggers the privileged rebase job, because the condition tests only that the thread is a pull request and that the comment body contains `/rebase`, and never checks `github.event.comment.author_association` or the commenter's repository permission. The `issue_comment` event runs the workflow from the default branch with repository secrets and the `GITHUB_TOKEN` passed to cirrus-actions/rebase on line 19, which fetches the pull request head and force-pushes a rewritten branch.

An account with no repository permission causes a privileged, secrets-bearing job to run on demand and to force-push a rewritten branch, which both crosses the pipeline's authorization boundary and gives an attacker a repeatable trigger for any weakness that later appears in that job.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `rebase.yml:10` gates the job only on `github.event.issue.pull_request != '' && contains(github.event.comment.body, '/rebase')`. The workflow declares no permissions block and passes `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN` to the rebase action on line 19, and no workflow under .github/workflows references `author_association`.

```yaml
// .github/workflows/rebase.yml:10
  rebase:
    name: Rebase
    if: github.event.issue.pull_request != '' && contains(github.event.comment.body, '/rebase')
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-039](#m-039) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (`rebase.yml:10`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-040"></a><a id="f-040"></a>
#### F-040 · Client-side-only authorization guard (frontend/src/app/app.guard.ts:54)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Angular SPA  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/app.guard.ts:54`

**Issue:** An attacker edits the `localStorage` 'token' entry to a JWT whose payload claims `data.role` = `'admin'`, and AdminGuard admits the route because `tokenDecode()` uses `jwtDecode`, which reads the payload without verifying the signature; the administration and accounting views and their privileged API-calling code then load in the attacker's browser. Any `user` can render the administration and accounting interfaces and trigger their privileged requests, so protection of `user` records, feedback deletion, and order `data` rests entirely on server-side checks the SPA neither performs nor verifies.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `app.guard.ts:35-38` reads the token from `localStorage` and decodes it with `jwtDecode` (a decode-only helper, no key or verification), and lines 54, 70, and 86 make the admin, accounting, and deluxe decisions solely from that unverified payload; `app.routing.ts:81` attaches AdminGuard as the only protection on the `/administration` route.

**Fix:** ◕ [M-040](#m-040) — Enforce authorization on the server (`app.guard.ts:54`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

### 🟡 Medium (29)

<a id="t-003"></a><a id="f-003"></a>
#### F-003 · Missing Security Event Logging

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (6)

**Instances (6):** `routes/login.ts:24`, `routes/chat.ts:184`, `routes/login.ts:50`, `lib/challengeUtils.ts:33`, `models/index.ts:42`, `routes/checkKeys.ts:18`

**Issue:** An attacker who brute-forces or injects their way into an account leaves no attributable trace, because `routes/login.ts:24` issues a session token without writing any security log entry, and the failure branch returns 401 with no record either. A repository-wide search for audit-log identifiers across lib and routes returned zero hits, and neither `routes/login.ts` nor `routes/resetPassword.ts` imports the logger at all, so successful logins, failed logins, and `password` resets cannot be reconstructed after the fact.

Account takeover via the login SQL injection or JWT forgery cannot be detected, timed, or attributed during incident response.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/login.ts:24` mints and stores a session token with no accompanying log call, and a zero-hit search confirms no audit-logging facility exists in lib or routes.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-003](#m-003) — Add security audit logging (`login.ts:24`)

**Classification:** Missing Audit Logging & Accountability · STRIDE: Repudiation · [CWE-778](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/778.html) · [OWASP A09:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A09_2025-Security_Logging_and_Alerting_Failures/)

<a id="t-041"></a><a id="f-041"></a>
#### F-041 · Unverified wallet ownership claim (routes/nftMint.ts:41)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/nftMint.ts:41`

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → api-server · Authentication: The boundary assumes every request crossing it carries an authenticated identity, but this handler derives the acting wallet identity from the request body alone.

**Issue:** An attacker reads any minter address from the public Sepolia NFT contract, then POSTs that address as `walletAddress` to `/rest/web3/walletNFTVerify`. The handler takes the wallet identity straight from the request body and looks it up in `addressesMinted` without ever asking the caller to sign a nonce with the corresponding private key, so the attacker is credited with a mint performed by a wallet it does not control.

On-chain actions performed by one wallet are attributed to whoever names that address, so the component's ownership-verification boundary conveys no assurance about who controls a wallet.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/nftMint.ts:41` assigns the wallet identity directly from `req.body.walletAddress`; a zero-hit search across all four component route files shows no signature-recovery or SIWE verification anywhere in the component.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-041](#m-041) — Require a signed nonce challenge before accepting walletAddress in routes/nftMi… (`nftMint.ts:41`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-290](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/290.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-042"></a><a id="f-042"></a>
#### F-042 · Open redirect via substring allowlist (routes/redirect.ts:19)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/redirect.ts:19`

**Issue:** An attacker appends an allowlisted URL as a query parameter of its own host, for example `/redirect?to`=`https://evil.example/?r=https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop`. `security.isRedirectAllowed` accepts the value because it tests `url.includes(allowedUrl)` rather than an exact or origin match, and `routes/redirect.ts:19` then issues a 302 to the attacker host from the application's own trusted origin.

The application lends its own origin to attacker-chosen destinations, enabling credible phishing against shop users and leakage of referrer-borne parameters to a third-party host.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/redirect.ts:19` calls `res.redirect(toUrl)` with the raw query value after a guard that `lib/insecurity.ts:136` implements as `url.includes(allowedUrl)`, which matches the allowlisted string at any position in the attacker's URL.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-042](#m-042) — Validate redirect targets against an allowlist (`redirect.ts:19`)

**Classification:** Open Redirect · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-043"></a><a id="f-043"></a>
#### F-043 · Remote installer executed without integrity check (.github/workflows/ci.yml:358)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:358`

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Issue:** An attacker who controls the cli-assets.heroku.com distribution point serves a modified `install.sh`, which `.github/workflows/ci.yml:358` pipes straight into sh with no checksum, signature, or pinned version. The script executes in the heroku job that goes on to deploy production with `secrets.HEROKU_API_KEY` on line 371, so the substituted code both runs on the runner and can read the deployment credential.

Substituted installer code runs on the deployment runner and reaches the Heroku API key, which deploys the master branch to the production juice-shop application, so a single upstream compromise reaches the production deployment.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `ci.yml:358` runs `curl https://cli-assets.heroku.com/install.sh | sh` with no checksum verification, signature check, or version pin, inside the same job that consumes `secrets.HEROKU_API_KEY` at line 371 to deploy to Heroku.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-043](#m-043) — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions (`ci.yml:358`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-044"></a><a id="f-044"></a>
#### F-044 · Seed download without integrity check (data/datacreator.ts:390)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - SQLite Database  ·  **Location:** `data/datacreator.ts:390`

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** An attacker who controls the configured product image or blueprint URL, or who intercepts the fetch, supplies arbitrary bytes that the seeding routine writes straight into the served asset directory, because `downloadToFile` is called with no checksum, signature, or content-type verification. Attacker-chosen content is published from the application's own asset path and the product catalog rows seeded alongside it reference it, so users receive tampered files from a trusted origin.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `data/datacreator.ts:390` calls `utils.downloadToFile` with a remote URL and a destination under frontend/dist/frontend/assets/public/images/products/ whose filename comes from that same URL, and line 418 repeats the pattern for the blueprint file; neither call verifies the retrieved bytes.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-044](#m-044) — Verify token signatures before trusting claims (`datacreator.ts:390`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-045"></a><a id="f-045"></a>
#### F-045 · Missing Content Security Policy (frontend/src/index.html:9)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Angular SPA  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/index.html:9`

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Vulnerable & Outdated Dependencies is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** An attacker exploiting any of the confirmed HTML-injection sinks faces no script-source restriction, because the SPA shell declares no `Content-Security-Policy`: a grep of frontend/src for a CSP or any http-equiv meta directive returns zero hits, so injected inline handlers and external script loads both execute, and the page can also be framed for clickjacking. Every injection finding in this component escalates from limited HTML to full script execution with external exfiltration, and no frame-ancestors directive limits embedding of the storefront in an attacker page.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `frontend/src/index.html:9-16` declares only charset, description, viewport, preconnect and favicon meta/link tags; a zero-hit search for 'content-security-policy|http-equiv' across frontend/src proves no policy is declared in the delivered document.

**Fix:** Add the missing protection mechanism for this surface (CSP / CSRF token / headers) → ◑ [M-045](#m-045) — Declare a script-src and frame-ancestors policy for the SPA shell (`index.html:9`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-693](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/693.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-046"></a><a id="f-046"></a>
#### F-046 · Unsigned CI commit to trunk (.github/workflows/frontend-bundle-analysis.yml:61)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/frontend-bundle-analysis.yml:61`

**Issue:** The bundle-analysis job sets its committer to a synthetic `GitHub Action <action@github.com>` identity and then commits and pushes to master on line 66 without a commit signature. An attacker with any write path into the repository can commit under identical author and committer fields, and a reviewer reading the master history has no signature or account link that distinguishes the pipeline's commit from the forged one.

Commits reaching the trunk cannot be attributed to an accountable principal, so a forensic review after a suspected pipeline compromise cannot separate genuine automated commits from forged ones, and a rejected push produces no failing run to investigate.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `frontend-bundle-analysis.yml:61-62` configures a committer name and e-mail that map to no GitHub account, line 66 pushes that commit to master, and line 67 marks the push step continue-on-error. No workflow under .github/workflows configures GPG or SSH commit signing.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-046](#m-046) — Add security audit logging (`frontend-bundle-analysis.yml:61`)

**Classification:** Missing Audit Logging & Accountability · STRIDE: Repudiation · [CWE-778](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/778.html) · [OWASP A09:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A09_2025-Security_Logging_and_Alerting_Failures/)

<a id="t-047"></a><a id="f-047"></a>
#### F-047 · Forgeable identity header (frontend/src/app/Services/request.interceptor.ts:23)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Angular SPA  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/Services/request.interceptor.ts:23`

**Issue:** A `user` opens developer tools, rewrites the `localStorage` 'email' entry to another person's address, and every subsequent API call carries X-User-Email with that forged value, because the interceptor copies browser-writable storage into an identity header on all outgoing requests; any server-side record or decision keyed on that header then names the wrong account. Actions logged or attributed by X-User-Email cannot be tied back to the account that actually performed them, so a `user` can repudiate purchases, complaints, or `data` changes and investigators cannot reconstruct who acted.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `request.interceptor.ts:20-25` reads `localStorage.getItem('email')` and sets it as the X-User-Email header on every request, and `login.component.ts:135` shows that value is written from the `user`'s own form input, never from a server-verified source.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-047](#m-047) — Stop sending a client-asserted identity header from (`request.interceptor.ts:23`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Repudiation · [CWE-290](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/290.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-048"></a><a id="f-048"></a>
#### F-048 · Confidential policy in LLM system prompt (routes/chat.ts:105)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:105`

**Issue:** LLM07 - System Prompt Leakage: `routes/chat.ts:105` places a rule marked `CONFIDENTIAL - INTERNAL ONLY` describing an undisclosed 15 percent courtesy discount into the `system` prompt that `buildSystemPrompt` returns and `routes/chat.ts:205` sends to the external provider on every request. Because the message array is attacker-controlled (`routes/chat.ts:191`) and the endpoint is unauthenticated `at ``server.ts:638`, any caller can instruct the model to repeat its instructions and recover the confidential clause together with the full coupon policy.

An undisclosed discount concession and the internal coupon-approval criteria leak to any anonymous caller, who can then demand the concession directly.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/chat.ts:105` embeds a business rule explicitly labelled confidential into the `system` prompt string that is transmitted to the LLM provider on every chat request.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-048](#m-048) — Stop exposing internal information to clients (`chat.ts:105`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-049"></a><a id="f-049"></a>
#### F-049 · Tool-call arguments streamed to all callers (routes/chat.ts:228)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:228`

**Issue:** LLM02 - Sensitive Information Disclosure: `routes/chat.ts:228` writes every tool-call event, including the tool name and the JSON-serialised model input, into the SSE response for every caller. The only `role` and cookie test in that branch is the `challengeUtils.solveIf` predicate, which merely records progress and does not gate the write, so the `show_tool_calls` cookie and the admin `role` comparison have no effect on what is emitted.

The internal tool surface and per-call arguments are exposed to anonymous callers, mapping out the coupon and order tools that the injection findings then target.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/chat.ts:228` executes unconditionally inside the tool-call case; the adjacent `role` and cookie comparison at line 225 sits inside a challenge predicate, not a conditional guard around the write.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-049](#m-049) — Stop exposing internal information to clients (`chat.ts:228`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-050"></a><a id="f-050"></a>
#### F-050 · Unauthenticated admin configuration endpoint (server.ts:607)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:607`

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → api-server · Authentication: The boundary assumes every protected route sits behind `expressJwt`, but the admin configuration route is registered with no authentication middleware at all.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-1 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Issue:** An anonymous caller retrieves the application's administrative configuration because `server.ts:607` registers `GET /rest/admin/application-configuration` with only `utils.asyncHandler(retrieveAppConfiguration())` and no authorization middleware. No `app.use('/rest/admin', ...)` guard exists anywhere in the router, and the neighbouring version endpoint is equally open, so the whole `/rest/admin` prefix is reachable without a token.

Deployment configuration and application version `data` are disclosed to anonymous callers, giving an attacker precise reconnaissance for follow-on exploitation.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:607` registers the admin configuration route with a bare handler; no `security.isAuthorized()` or `role` guard appears on that path or on any `/rest/admin` prefix.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-050](#m-050) — Stop exposing internal information to clients (`server.ts:607`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-051"></a><a id="f-051"></a>
#### F-051 · Stack traces returned by error handler (server.ts:682)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:682`

**Weakness:** [W-012](#w-012) - Sensitive Data Exposure is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** An attacker harvests stack traces, file paths, and library versions because `server.ts:682` mounts `errorhandler()` as the terminal Express middleware with no environment guard, and `server.ts:730` sets its title from the application name and Express version. Every route that forwards a failure through `next(error)` - for example the raw SQL failure path `at ``routes/login.ts:53` - reaches this handler, so a malformed injection payload returns the underlying SQLite error and the server-side call stack to the caller.

Internal file paths, dependency versions, and database error text are disclosed to anonymous callers, accelerating discovery of the injection and authentication flaws in the same component.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:682` registers the `errorhandler` middleware unconditionally, with no `NODE_ENV` or production check anywhere on that registration.

**Fix:** Replace developer error pages with a generic message in production responses → ◑ [M-051](#m-051) — Return generic error messages to clients (`server.ts:682`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-209](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/209.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-052"></a><a id="f-052"></a>
#### F-052 · Password in URL query string (routes/changePassword.ts:15)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Authentication and Session Service  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:15`

**Issue:** A `user` changes their `password` and the current and new plaintext passwords travel in the request URL, because `routes/changePassword.ts` reads them from `query.current` and `query.new`; anyone with access to reverse-proxy access logs, browser history, or a `Referer` header captured by a third-party asset on the page recovers those credentials verbatim. Plaintext passwords are persisted in infrastructure logs and browser history outside the application's control, so a log reader or a compromised third-party script recovers working credentials for the affected accounts.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/changePassword.ts:15` reads the new `password` from `query.new` and line 14 reads the current `password` from `query.current`, so both credentials are carried in the request line rather than a request body.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-052](#m-052) — Move password-change credentials from the query string to the request body (`changePassword.ts:15`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-053"></a><a id="f-053"></a>
#### F-053 · Dockerfile USER directive (non-root)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1`

**Issue:** `Dockerfile` USER directive (non-root): Containers running as root expand container-escape blast radius.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-053](#m-053) — Drop unnecessary privileges in build and runtime (`Dockerfile:1`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-054"></a><a id="f-054"></a>
#### F-054 · Container image signing via cosign or attest-build-provenance

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (16)

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Instances (16):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/frontend-bundle-analysis.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/lint-fixer.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/lock.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/pr-compliance.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/rebase.yml:1` … (+8 more)

**Issue:** Container image signing via cosign or `attest-build-provenance: Unsigned` container images cannot be verified for provenance - any registry intermediary could substitute them.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown `algorithms` → ◑ [M-054](#m-054) — Sign and verify release artifacts (`ci.yml:1`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-055"></a><a id="f-055"></a>
#### F-055 · Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Instances (2):** `Dockerfile:4`, `package.json:56`

**Issue:** `Dockerfile` npm/pnpm/yarn install without `--ignore-scripts`: Without `--ignore-scripts`, any compromised dependency publisher can execute postinstall code at build time.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-055](#m-055) — Disable untrusted package install scripts (`Dockerfile:4`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-056"></a><a id="f-056"></a>
#### F-056 · GitHub Actions workflow-level permissions block

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (16)

**Instances (16):** `.github/workflows/lock.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/pr-compliance.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/frontend-bundle-analysis.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/lint-fixer.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/rebase.yml:1` … (+8 more)

**Issue:** GitHub Actions workflow-level permissions block: Without an explicit permissions block, the workflow inherits the repository default (commonly write-all) for `GITHUB_TOKEN` - any compromised step can push code, create releases, or approve PRs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-056](#m-056) — Apply least-privilege permissions (`ci.yml:1`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-057"></a><a id="f-057"></a>
#### F-057 · Third-party GitHub Actions pinned to commit SHA

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Instances (2):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:188`, `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml:23`

**Issue:** Third-party GitHub Actions pinned to commit SHA: Tag-based action references (@v3, @main) are mutable - a compromised publisher can retroactively inject malicious code into an already-used tag.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-057](#m-057) — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions (`ci.yml:188`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-058"></a><a id="f-058"></a>
#### F-058 · Dockerfile base image must be digest-pinned

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Vulnerable & Outdated Dependencies is implemented inconsistently

**Instances (2):** `Dockerfile:1`, `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1`

**Issue:** `Dockerfile` base image must be `digest-pinned: Tag-only` base images (FROM node:24) can be silently substituted by a malicious publisher. Digest-pinning (@sha256:…) ensures the exact image bytes are used on every build.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace the unmaintained dependency with a maintained equivalent or fork it under ownership → ◑ [M-058](#m-058) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest (`Dockerfile:1`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-059"></a><a id="f-059"></a>
#### F-059 · Package-lock.json present and committed

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js API Server  ·  **Location:** `package-lock.json`

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Vulnerable & Outdated Dependencies is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** `package-lock.json` present and committed: Without a lockfile, `npm install` installs different transitive versions on each run - supply-chain attacks become undetectable.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace the unmaintained dependency with a maintained equivalent or fork it under ownership → ◑ [M-059](#m-059) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest (`package-lock.json:0`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-060"></a><a id="f-060"></a>
#### F-060 · Hardcoded test credentials (frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:62)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Angular SPA  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:62`

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker opens the shipped JavaScript bundle, reads the literal account name and `password` compiled into LoginComponent, and submits them to `/rest/user/login` to obtain a session without any guessing. Anyone who views the bundle obtains a working credential pair for the shop, giving whatever access that account holds without touching authentication controls such as rate limiting or lockout.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:61-62` declares public `testingUsername` = `'testing@juice-``sh.op``'` and public `testingPassword` = `'IamU**** (17 chars)'` as component fields, so both literals are compiled into the client bundle delivered to every visitor.

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-060](#m-060) — Move secrets to a managed secret store (`login.component.ts:62`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-061"></a><a id="f-061"></a>
#### F-061 · Session cookie without flags (frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:104)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Angular SPA  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:104`

**Weakness:** [W-013](#w-013) - Cross-Site Scripting is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** An attacker reads or replays the duplicate session cookie the SPA writes itself, because `cookieService.put('token', ...)` is called from JavaScript with only an expiry, so the cookie is by construction not HttpOnly and carries neither Secure nor `SameSite`, leaving it readable by injected script and attachable to cross-site requests. The session token exists in a second location that script can read and that the browser attaches to cross-site requests, widening both theft and cross-site request forgery exposure for authenticated actions.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:102-104` creates an eight-hour expiry and calls `this.cookieService.put('token', authentication.token, { expires })` with no secure, `httpOnly`, or `sameSite` option; `oauth.component.ts:50` repeats the same write.

**Fix:** Set `HttpOnly` on every session cookie → ◑ [M-061](#m-061) — Set secure session-cookie attributes (`login.component.ts:104`)

**Classification:** Insecure Client-Side Storage · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-1004](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1004.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-062"></a><a id="f-062"></a>
#### F-062 · Unbounded session token map (lib/insecurity.ts:74)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Authentication and Session Service  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:74`

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Denial of Service is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** An attacker repeatedly authenticates or replays distinct valid tokens; each one is inserted into the process-local `tokenMap`, which has no size cap, no expiry sweep and no removal on logout, so resident memory grows monotonically until the Node process is killed by the allocator. Sustained token insertion exhausts heap memory and terminates the authentication process, taking down login for all users; the same map also retains full `user` records for sessions that were never revoked.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/insecurity.ts:74` writes `this.tokenMap[token]` = `user` with no eviction, and `updateAuthenticatedUsers` at line 191 inserts any externally supplied token that verifies, including one presented through the token cookie read at line 187.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-062](#m-062) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`insecurity.ts:74`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-063"></a><a id="f-063"></a>
#### F-063 · Unbounded CI fan-out on pull requests (.github/workflows/ci.yml:14)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Denial of Service is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** Any internet `user` can open and then repeatedly update a pull request against the public repository, and the `pull_request` trigger starts frontend-test, server-test and api-test, each a three-operating-system by three-Node-version matrix whose test steps retry up to three times, alongside the e2e, smoke, docker-test and custom-config jobs. The workflow declares no concurrency group, so each new commit the attacker pushes queues another full set of runners instead of cancelling the previous run.

Actions concurrency and billed minutes for the juice-shop organisation are exhausted by attacker-controlled pull request activity, delaying or blocking the release, Docker publish and Heroku deploy jobs that depend on the same runner pool.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `ci.yml:14` registers the `pull_request` trigger with no fork or author restriction, lines 56-59, 95-98 and 132-135 define three-by-three matrices for three separate jobs, the nick-invision/retry steps allow three attempts each, and the file contains no concurrency key at workflow or job level.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-063](#m-063) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`ci.yml:14`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-064"></a><a id="f-064"></a>
#### F-064 · Unbounded regex on socket input (lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:46)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:46`

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Denial of Service is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** An attacker opens many anonymous sockets and floods `verifySvgInjectionChallenge` with maximum-size payloads. Each event runs a backtracking regular expression carrying a leading `.*` and a lazy `[\w/-]*?` quantifier over the whole attacker-controlled string, synchronously on the single Node event loop that also serves the REST API on port 3000.

A single anonymous client can saturate the Node event loop of the process that also serves the REST API on port 3000, degrading or stalling the whole application for all users without authenticating.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:46` evaluates `data?.match(/.*\.\.\/\.\.\/\.\.[\w/-]*?\/redirect\?to=https?:\/\/cataas.com\/cat.*/)` directly on the client-supplied payload with no length check. A grep of both component files for `maxHttpBufferSize`, `rateLimit`, `rate-limit`, and `throttle` returned zero hits, and line 20 shows the Socket\.IO server is constructed with `cors` as its only option, so the default message size governs and no event rate limit exists.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-064](#m-064) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:46`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-065"></a><a id="f-065"></a>
#### F-065 · Unbounded persisted text growth (models/feedback.ts:40)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - SQLite Database  ·  **Location:** `models/feedback.ts:40`

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Denial of Service is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** An attacker submits very large comment bodies repeatedly and grows the single SQLite file until the host filesystem fills, because the comment column declares no length validator and SQLite does not enforce the declared string type, so nothing bounds the bytes written per row. A full filesystem makes the shared database unwritable for every `user` and the application cannot recover on its own, taking down ordering, authentication, and all state held in the store.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `models/feedback.ts:39-56` declares comment as `DataTypes.STRING` with a sanitizing setter but no validate block, while `models/address.ts:55` and :61 show the project's own len validator pattern being applied elsewhere; the store is a single file `at ``models/index.ts:41` and the retry policy `at ``models/index.ts:36` matches only `SQLITE_BUSY`.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-065](#m-065) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`feedback.ts:40`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-066"></a><a id="f-066"></a>
#### F-066 · Unbounded in-memory set growth (routes/web3Wallet.ts:16)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/web3Wallet.ts:16`

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Denial of Service is implemented inconsistently

**Issue:** An attacker sends a stream of unauthenticated POSTs to `/rest/web3/walletExploitAddress`, each carrying a distinct `walletAddress` string. `routes/web3Wallet.ts:16` adds every value to the process-global `walletsConnected` set before any validation, and entries are removed only when a matching on-chain ContractExploited event arrives, so the set grows without any cap, eviction, or expiry until the Node process exhausts its heap.

A single unauthenticated client can drive the API server process to heap exhaustion, taking down the shared Express application rather than just the web3 endpoints.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/web3Wallet.ts:16` executes `walletsConnected.add(metamaskAddress)` on the request path with no size limit, TTL, or address-format check, and a zero-hit search for rate limiting across the component route files shows no request-rate bound on the endpoint.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-066](#m-066) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`web3Wallet.ts:16`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-067"></a><a id="f-067"></a>
#### F-067 · Default-scope workflow token (.github/workflows/release.yml:20)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:20`

**Issue:** The release workflow declares no permissions block at workflow or job level, so the `GITHUB_TOKEN` handed to softprops/action-gh-release and to Gamesight/slack-workflow-status on line 108 is issued with the repository's default scope rather than the `contents: write` that publishing a release needs. Any step in the packaging matrix that an attacker reaches, through the unlocked `npm install --production` on line 55 or a compromised action, inherits that broader write access to contents, packages, issues and pull requests.

A compromised release step operates with whatever the repository default token scope is rather than a scope the workflow can be reviewed against, so the blast radius of any other pipeline weakness cannot be bounded from the repository source alone.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `release.yml` declares no permissions key at workflow or job level while passing `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN` to third-party actions on lines 20, 63 and 108. Only `codeql-analysis.yml`, `pr-compliance.yml` and `lock.yml` under .github/workflows declare a permissions block; `ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `image_actions.yml`, `lint-fixer.yml`, `rebase.yml`, `frontend-bundle-analysis.yml`, `zap_scan.yml`, `stale.yml` and the five update-* workflows do not.

**Fix:** Add explicit server-side authorisation checks on every protected route → ◑ [M-067](#m-067) — Apply least-privilege filesystem access (`release.yml:20`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-068"></a><a id="f-068"></a>
#### F-068 · No database principal separation (models/index.ts:33)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - SQLite Database  ·  **Location:** `models/index.ts:33`

**Issue:** An attacker who reaches any query path, including one opened by an injection flaw elsewhere in the process, operates with full authority over every table, because a single Sequelize connection created once at module load serves all access levels and SQLite enforces no per-principal or row-level permission. There is no containment boundary inside the store: a flaw on any single query path yields read and write access to credentials, payment cards, addresses, orders, and wallet balances alike.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `createSequelize` `at ``models/index.ts:33-44` builds one connection against the file store and `initModels` binds all twenty-one entities to that single instance at line 75, so no read-only, per-role, or per-tenant connection exists anywhere in the persistence layer.

**Fix:** Add explicit server-side authorisation checks on every protected route → ◑ [M-068](#m-068) — Apply least-privilege filesystem access (`index.ts:33`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

## 9. Abuse Cases

_Abuse cases describe end-to-end attack scenarios that chain individual findings into an exploitation path. Each case is **mandatory** - defined in the org profile / plugin library and evaluated against every repository. Every chain step references a finding from [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register); each step is code-confirmed against the repository and the chain verdict is folded deterministically from the per-step results, never rated by hand._

| # | Scenario | Actor | Combined Risk | Verdict |
|--------|------------------------------------|------------------|-------------|--------------|
| [AC-T-001](#ac-t-001) | Account Takeover via Stored XSS + Token<br/>Hijacking | external-attacker | 🔴 Critical | ◐ Partially blocked |
| [AC-T-002](#ac-t-002) | Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object<br/>`Authorization` | authenticated-user | 🔴 Critical | ◐ Partially blocked |
| [AC-T-003](#ac-t-003) | Privilege Escalation to Admin via JWT<br/>Algorithm Confusion | external-attacker | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-004](#ac-t-004) | Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on<br/>Registration | external-attacker | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-005](#ac-t-005) | Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret<br/>Material | external-attacker | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-006](#ac-t-006) | Remote Code Execution via Server-Side<br/>Injection | external-attacker | 🟠 High | ✓ Mitigated |

_Verdict: ⚠ Fully viable - no effective control blocks this chain · ◐ Partially blocked - at least one step has a compensating control but the chain is not fully closed · ✓ Mitigated - chain is broken at a verified step · ? Inconclusive - could not be verified end-to-end._

---

<a id="ac-t-001"></a>
### AC-T-001 — Account Takeover via Stored XSS + Token Hijacking

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** external-attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ◐ Partially blocked

**Goal:** Obtain persistent authenticated access as an arbitrary `user` without valid credentials.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can submit content that is later rendered to other users (e.g. feedback, comments, profile fields).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) (`search-result.component.ts:143`) | Attacker JavaScript executes in the victim's<br/>browser session. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Session token in localStorage (`login.component.ts:101`) | Token exfiltrated from local/session storage<br/>via the Step 1 payload. |
| 3 | 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Implicit OAuth flow (`login.component.ts:148`) | Exfiltrated token accepted for a new<br/>session; absence of token binding / PKCE<br/>removes the last server-side revocation<br/>opportunity. |

**Why combined risk exceeds individual ratings**

Individually the XSS sink and the web-readable token storage rate below Critical, but chained they form a repeatable credential-theft path: a single stored payload causes indefinite session compromise for every `user` who views the affected page.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ● [M-009](#m-009) — Render the search term as text instead of trusted HTML in search-result.compone… (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ◕ [M-030](#m-030) — Move the session token out of localStorage into an HttpOnly cookie (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Session token in localStorage breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ◕ [M-015](#m-015) — Replace the implicit flow with authorization code plus PKCE in login.component.… (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Implicit OAuth flow breaks the chain at **Step 3**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-002"></a>
### AC-T-002 — Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** authenticated-user - authenticated low-privilege `user` · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ◐ Partially blocked

**Goal:** Enumerate and exfiltrate other users' records, then escalate own permissions via unguarded mass assignment.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker holds a valid, non-privileged `user` account.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Insecure Direct Object Reference (`wallet.ts:27`) | Attacker enumerates and retrieves records<br/>for arbitrary object IDs; no ownership<br/>comparison is performed. |
| 2 | 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body (`verify.ts:53`) | Update endpoint persists an unfiltered `role`<br/>(or equivalent) field supplied in the<br/>request body. |

**Why combined risk exceeds individual ratings**

The ownership gap exposes every record, and the mass-assignment gap lets the same low-privilege actor self-elevate - together they turn a single compromised account into full tenant `data` access and `role` escalation.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ● [M-008](#m-008) — Replace req.body.UserId/userId/ownerId with req.user.id… (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Insecure Direct Object Reference breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ● [M-010](#m-010) — Apply an allowlist filter (Joi/Zod/yup schema, `_.pick`, or explicit field copy)… (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-003"></a>
### AC-T-003 — Privilege Escalation to Admin via JWT Algorithm Confusion

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** external-attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge an admin-role JWT without knowledge of the signing secret.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can obtain any valid JWT issued by the `system` (e.g. by registering a free account).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — JWT Verification Without Algorithm Allowlist (`insecurity.ts:189`) | Verifier accepts attacker-chosen `alg` (e.g.<br/>`none` or HMAC-with-public-key), allowing<br/>token re-signing without the secret. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-036](#f-036) — Deluxe role granted without payment (`deluxe.ts:43`) | Forged `role: admin` claim is accepted as<br/>authoritative because the `role` is not<br/>re-fetched from the database per request. |

**Why combined risk exceeds individual ratings**

Algorithm confusion alone yields a forgeable token; trusting the in-token `role` claim turns that forgery into instant admin access - neither gap is Critical in isolation, but the chain is a full authentication bypass.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ● [M-001](#m-001) — Pin the accepted JWT algorithm to RS256 in `lib/insecurity.ts` (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — JWT Verification Without Algorithm Allowlist breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ◕ [M-036](#m-036) — Require a completed payment before the role update in `routes/deluxe.ts` (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-036](#f-036) — Deluxe role granted without payment breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-004"></a>
### AC-T-004 — Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on Registration

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** external-attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Obtain an administrator account without any existing privilege.

**Prerequisite:** Self-registration is open (one unauthenticated POST).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body (`verify.ts:53`) | The account-creation handler persists the<br/>request body wholesale, so a client-supplied<br/>`role` (or `isAdmin`) field is written verbatim. |

**Why combined risk exceeds individual ratings**

A single unauthenticated request mints an admin account when the registration handler trusts a client-supplied `role` field - the most direct full-compromise path in role-based apps with open sign-up.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ● [M-010](#m-010) — Apply an allowlist filter (Joi/Zod/yup schema, `_.pick`, or explicit field copy)… (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-005"></a>
### AC-T-005 — Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** external-attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge trusted tokens / credentials and impersonate any `user`.

**Prerequisite:** Signing material or other secrets are reachable (committed to a public repo, served by an unauthenticated route, or in an exposed directory).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Hardcoded RSA signing key in source (`insecurity.ts:21`) | A private key, signing secret, or credential<br/>file is committed to the source repository<br/>or served without authentication. |
| 2 | 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — JWT Verification Without Algorithm Allowlist (`insecurity.ts:189`) | The exposed key/secret is the same one the<br/>server trusts, so a token signed with it (or<br/>the leaked credential) is accepted as<br/>authentic. |

**Why combined risk exceeds individual ratings**

Exposed signing material collapses the entire authentication boundary: any attacker who reads the key can mint a valid token for any identity or `role`, with no credential ever required.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ● [M-004](#m-004) — Load the JWT signing key from external secret configuration in `lib/insecurity.ts` (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Hardcoded RSA signing key in source breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ● [M-001](#m-001) — Pin the accepted JWT algorithm to RS256 in `lib/insecurity.ts` (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — JWT Verification Without Algorithm Allowlist breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-006"></a>
### AC-T-006 — Remote Code Execution via Server-Side Injection

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** external-attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ✓ Mitigated

**Goal:** Execute arbitrary code in the application process.

**Prerequisite:** An input reaches a server-side interpreter / template / `eval`.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|---------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | _no matching finding_<br/>`lib/xml.ts:38` | Attacker-controlled input is passed to `eval`,<br/>a server-side template engine, an unsafe<br/>sandbox, or an unsafe deserializer. |

**Why combined risk exceeds individual ratings**

A single injection into a server-side interpreter yields code execution in the application process - the highest-impact outcome, granting full filesystem and network access from one unauthenticated request.

### P1 — Immediate

<a id="m-001"></a>
#### M-001 — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — JWT Verification Without Algorithm Allowlist (`insecurity.ts:189`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:189`

**How:**

1. Pass `algorithms: ['RS256']` to the `jwt.verify` call `at ``lib/insecurity.ts:189` and to the `expressJwt` configuration `at ``lib/insecurity.ts:52` so a token header can never select the accepted algorithm.
2. Add a negative test that signs a token `HS256` with the contents of `encryptionkeys/jwt.pub` as the secret and asserts that a protected route returns 401.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:189`: it applies **Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Always verify on the public key; never trust the unsigned header.
const decoded = jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'] })
```

**Verification:** Run the new test: `npm test -- --grep 'HS256 confusion'`; the request carrying the `HS256`-forged admin token must return HTTP 401 and no `user` context.

**Reference:** [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html): Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

---

<a id="m-004"></a>
#### M-004 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Hardcoded RSA signing key in source (`insecurity.ts:21`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**How:**

1. Replace the literal `at ``lib/insecurity.ts:21` with a value read from an environment variable or secret store at startup, and fail startup when it is absent rather than falling back to a default.
2. Rotate the compromised key pair, regenerate `encryptionkeys/jwt.pub`, and add a startup test asserting the process exits non-zero when the signing key variable is unset.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:21`: it applies **Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Load the RSA private key from an environment variable / KMS — never
// the source tree. Rotate the prior key and revoke outstanding tokens.
const privateKey = process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY
if (!privateKey) throw new Error('JWT_PRIVATE_KEY not set')
const token = jwt.sign(claims, privateKey, { algorithm: 'RS256' })
```

**Verification:** Start the server with the signing-key variable unset and assert a non-zero exit; then grep the repository for `BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY` and expect zero hits outside test fixtures.

**Reference:** [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html): Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

---

<a id="m-005"></a>
#### M-005 — Move secrets to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hard-coded seeded account credentials (`datacreator.ts:196`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) - Use of Hard-coded Credentials

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `data/datacreator.ts:196`

**How:**

1. In `createUsers` (`data/datacreator.ts:186-202`), stop persisting the static `password` field: require each seeded account `password` from external configuration at startup, or generate one per account with `crypto.randomBytes` and disclose it once through an operator-only channel.
2. Add a seeding test that asserts no persisted User row hashes a `password` value present in the static `user` `data` file.

_Example implementation in `data/datacreator.ts:196`: it applies **Move secrets to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Read secrets from env / vault, not the source.
const hmacKey = process.env.ORDER_HMAC_KEY
if (!hmacKey) throw new Error('ORDER_HMAC_KEY not set')
const sig = createHmac('sha256', hmacKey).update(payload).digest('hex')
```

**Verification:** Run the seeding routine twice against fresh databases and compare the two admin `password` column values; they must differ, and neither may equal the `hash` of the static configured `password`.

**Reference:** [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html): Use of Hard-coded Credentials

---

<a id="m-006"></a>
#### M-006 — Use OAuth authorization code flow with PKCE

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-006](#f-006) — Predictable derived password (`oauth.component.ts:30`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-522](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/522.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**How:**

1. Remove the `btoa(reversed email)` `password` derivation `at ``frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30` and :46 and let the server establish the federated session from the verified token alone, with no local `password` created for the account.
2. Where a local credential must exist for legacy reasons, have the server generate it with a CSPRNG and never return or reconstruct it in the browser.
3. Add a spec asserting that OAuthComponent issues no `userService.save()` call carrying a `password` field.

**Verification:** Run `npx vitest run frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.spec.ts`; assert that the request body captured for `/api/Users` contains no `password` property and that login is driven by the OAuth token only.

**Reference:** [CWE-522: Insufficiently Protected Credentials](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/522.html)

---

<a id="m-007"></a>
#### M-007 — Use parameterized database queries

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — SQL Injection (`login.ts:34`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**How:**

1. Rewrite `routes/login.ts:34` as a `UserModel.findOne({ where: { email, password: hashedPassword, deletedAt: null } })` call so Sequelize binds both values instead of interpolating them.
2. Add a negative test posting `' OR 1=1--` as the email and asserting HTTP 401 with no authentication payload.

_Example implementation in `routes/login.ts:34`: it applies **Use parameterized database queries**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Reject string interpolation; use parameter binding.
await sequelize.query(
  'SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = :email AND password = :password',
  { replacements: { email, password: hash(password) }, type: QueryTypes.SELECT }
)
```

**Verification:** POST `/rest/user/login` with `{"email":"' OR 1=1--","password":"x"}` and expect HTTP 401 and a body containing no `authentication.token` field.

**Reference:** [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html): Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command

---

<a id="m-008"></a>
#### M-008 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Insecure Direct Object Reference (`wallet.ts:27`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - `Authorization` Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/wallet.ts:27`

**How:** Replace `req.body.UserId`/`userId`/`ownerId` with `req.user.id`…

1. Replace `req.body.UserId`/`userId`/`ownerId` with `req.user.id` (or equivalent session-derived identity) in every WHERE/filter clause.
2. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

_Example implementation in `routes/wallet.ts:27`: it applies **Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Ownership check before touching a resource.
const basket = await Basket.findByPk(req.params.id)
if (!basket || basket.UserId !== req.user.id) return res.status(403).end()
```

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHZ-001 scanner check; it reports no match `at ``routes/wallet.ts:27`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-009"></a>
#### M-009 — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) (`search-result.component.ts:143`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143`

**How:**

1. Remove the `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` call `at ``frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143` and keep `searchValue` a plain string.
2. Change `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.html:11` from [`innerHTML`]="searchValue" to an interpolated {{ `searchValue` }} binding so Angular escapes the value.
3. Replace the assertion in `search-result.component.spec.ts` that currently expects `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` to be called with '`<script>``alert("XSS")``</script>`' by one asserting the rendered #`searchValue` element contains the payload as text and no script node.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143`: it applies **Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Never call bypassSecurityTrust*; let Angular sanitize.
// template:  <div [innerHTML]="product.description"></div>
// component: no DomSanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(...)
this.product.description = raw  // bound directly; Angular escapes
```

**Verification:** Run `npx vitest run frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.spec.ts`; loading `q`=`<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` must leave `querySelector('#searchValue img')` null and the escaped text present.

**Reference:** [CWE-79: Improper Neutralization of Input During Web Page Generation (XSS)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html)

---

<a id="m-010"></a>
#### M-010 — Allowlist client-controlled fields

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body (`verify.ts:53`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/verify.ts:53`

**How:** Apply an allowlist filter (Joi/Zod/yup schema, `_.pick`, or explicit field copy)…

1. Apply an allowlist filter (Joi/Zod/yup schema, `_.pick`, or explicit field copy) before passing the body to any model, and strip privilege fields before persistence.
2. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

_Example implementation in `routes/verify.ts:53`: it applies **Allowlist client-controlled fields**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Whitelist mutable fields server-side; never trust the body.
const ALLOWED = ['email', 'username'] as const
const patch = Object.fromEntries(
  Object.entries(req.body).filter(([k]) => ALLOWED.includes(k as any))
)
await user.update(patch)
```

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHZ-003 scanner check; it reports no match `at ``routes/verify.ts:53`, and the regression test passes.

```typescript
  next()
})
```

<a id="m-013"></a>
#### M-013 — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`

**How:**

1. Register an `io.use()` `handshake` middleware in `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts` that reads the token from `socket.handshake.auth` and verifies it with the same JWT verification helper `lib/insecurity.ts` supplies to `security.isAuthorized()`, calling `next(new Error('unauthorized'))` when verification fails so the connection is refused before `io.on('connection')` runs.
2. Attach the verified principal to `socket.data` and make the event handlers below it read identity from there instead of from client payloads.
3. Replace the hardcoded `http://localhost:4200` origin with a configured allow-list, and keep the origin check as defence in depth rather than as the admission control.
4. Add an integration test that opens a socket\.io-client connection with no token and with a tampered token, and asserts the connection is rejected and no 'challenge `solved`' event is received.

**Verification:** Run `npx wscat -c 'ws://localhost:3000/socket.io/?EIO=4&transport=websocket'` with no auth payload and confirm the server closes the connection with an unauthorized error instead of emitting 'server started' or 'challenge `solved`'.

**Reference:** [CWE-306: Missing Authentication for Critical Function](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)

**Addresses:**

**Prevents CWEs:**

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

<a id="m-023"></a>
#### M-023 — Validate and allowlist outbound request targets

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-023](#f-023) — SSRF via profile image URL (`profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) - Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`

**How:**

1. Before the fetch `at ``routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`, parse the URL, require an https scheme, resolve the host, and reject loopback, link-local, and private address ranges as well as any host outside a configured allowlist.
2. Set an explicit request timeout and response size cap on the fetch, and stop persisting the raw URL in the failure branch at line 36.
3. Add tests posting `http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/` and `http://127.0.0.1:3000/` and asserting both are rejected before any outbound request.

_Example implementation in `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`: it applies **Validate and allowlist outbound request targets**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Allowlist external image hosts; block private IP ranges.
const ALLOWED_HOSTS = new Set(['images.example.com', 'cdn.example.com'])
const url = new URL(input)
if (!ALLOWED_HOSTS.has(url.hostname)) throw new Error('host not allowed')
if (/^(10\.|172\.(1[6-9]|2[0-9]|3[01])\.|192\.168\.|127\.)/.test(url.hostname))
  throw new Error('private range blocked')
```

**Verification:** POST `/profile/image/url` with `imageUrl=http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/` and assert HTTP 400, no outbound socket in the test spy, and an unchanged `profileImage` field.

**Reference:** [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html): Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)

<a id="m-029"></a>
#### M-029 — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Unsalted fast password hash (`user.ts:76`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-009](#w-009)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `models/user.ts:76`

**How:**

1. Change the `password` setter `at ``models/user.ts:76` to store a bcrypt or Argon2id `hash` with per-user salt and tuned cost parameters, reject `password` input above the algorithm's byte limit, and rehash on next successful login for existing rows.
2. Add a model test asserting two users with the same cleartext `password` store different column values and that the stored value carries the KDF identifier and cost parameters.

_Example implementation in `models/user.ts:76`: it applies **Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Replace md5/sha1 password hashes with bcrypt or argon2.
import bcrypt from 'bcrypt'
const hash = await bcrypt.hash(plaintext, 12)
const ok = await bcrypt.compare(plaintext, storedHash)
```

**Verification:** Create two users with the same `password` and confirm their stored `password` columns differ and both begin with the KDF prefix.

**Reference:** [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html): Use of Password Hash With Insufficient Computational Effort

<a id="m-030"></a>
#### M-030 — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Session token in localStorage (`login.component.ts:101`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) - Insecure Storage of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** High · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`

**How:**

1. Have the API set the session token as an HttpOnly, Secure, `SameSite=Strict` cookie at login and delete the `localStorage` writes `at ``frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`, `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:51`, and `frontend/src/app/two-factor-auth-enter/two-factor-auth-enter.component.ts:53`.
2. Drop the `Authorization` header logic in `frontend/src/app/Services/request.interceptor.ts:13-19` in favour of `withCredentials`, and add CSRF protection for state-changing calls now authenticated by an ambient cookie.
3. Add specs asserting that no login path writes a token to `localStorage` and that state-changing requests carry the CSRF token.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`: it applies **Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Move the JWT out of localStorage into an httpOnly cookie.
res.cookie('session', token, {
  httpOnly: true, secure: true, sameSite: 'lax', maxAge: 3600_000
})
```

**Verification:** Run `npx vitest run frontend/src/app/login/login.component.spec.ts frontend/src/app/Services/request.interceptor.spec.ts`; after a successful login `localStorage.getItem('token')` must be null and requests must be issued with `withCredentials` true.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: HTML5 Security Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/HTML5_Security_Cheat_Sheet.html#local-storage)

<a id="m-031"></a>
#### M-031 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-031](#f-031) — Hardcoded wallet mnemonic in source (`checkKeys.ts:10`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`

**How:**

1. Load the mnemonic from a required environment variable or secret manager in `routes/checkKeys.ts`, fail startup when it is absent, generate a fresh mnemonic and migrate any assets off the exposed wallet, and purge the literal from source.
2. Add a startup test asserting the process exits with a configuration error when the mnemonic variable is unset, and a repository check asserting no BIP-39 phrase literal remains under routes/.

_Example implementation in `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`: it applies **Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Load the RSA private key from an environment variable / KMS — never
// the source tree. Rotate the prior key and revoke outstanding tokens.
const privateKey = process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY
if (!privateKey) throw new Error('JWT_PRIVATE_KEY not set')
const token = jwt.sign(claims, privateKey, { algorithm: 'RS256' })
```

**Verification:** git grep -n 'purpose betray marriage' -- routes/ must return no matches, and starting the server without the mnemonic environment variable must fail with a configuration error rather than boot.

**Reference:** [CWE-321: Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html)
