<a id="m-032"></a>
#### M-032 — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-032](#f-032) — Login endpoint without rate limiting (`server.ts:596`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) - Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `server.ts:596`

**How:**

1. Insert a `rateLimit` middleware ahead of `login()` `at ``server.ts:596` keyed on both the submitted email and the true client address, backed by a shared store so the limit survives multiple processes.
2. Add progressive delay or temporary lockout after repeated failures for the same account, and emit a throttling event to the audit sink.
3. Add a test issuing repeated failed logins for one account and asserting HTTP 429 once the threshold is crossed.

_Example implementation in `server.ts:596`: it applies **Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Per-IP + per-account rate limiting on auth endpoints.
import rateLimit from 'express-rate-limit'
app.use('/rest/user/login',
  rateLimit({ windowMs: 60_000, max: 5, standardHeaders: true }))
```

**Verification:** Issue login attempts above the configured threshold for one email from one source and assert the excess requests return HTTP 429 and no database query is executed.

**Reference:** [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html): Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

<a id="m-033"></a>
#### M-033 — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-033](#f-033) — Unbounded LLM token consumption (`server.ts:638`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) - Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `server.ts:638`

**How:**

1. Add `security.isAuthorized()` and a per-identity `rateLimit` middleware to the route registration `at ``server.ts:638` so anonymous callers cannot reach the provider at all.
2. Set an explicit `maxOutputTokens` on the `streamText` call `at ``routes/chat.ts:203` and reject request bodies whose total message length exceeds a configured character budget.
3. Add a test that posts an oversized message array and asserts HTTP 413 before any provider call is attempted.

**Verification:** POST `/rest/chat` anonymously and assert HTTP 401; then post an over-budget message array with a valid token and assert HTTP 413 with the provider client spy showing zero calls.

**Reference:** OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications 2025 - LLM10: Unbounded Consumption

<a id="m-034"></a>
#### M-034 — Key the reset-password limiter on the proxy-validated address

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Rate limiter bypassable via forwarded header (`server.ts:346`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-693](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/693.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:346`

**How:**

1. Remove the custom `keyGenerator` `at ``server.ts:346` so `express-rate-limit` uses `req.ip`, and configure `app.set('trust proxy', ...)` `at ``server.ts:342` with the specific hop count or address of the deployment's reverse proxy instead of the blanket enable.
2. Add a second limiter dimension keyed on the submitted email so one account cannot be attacked from many sources.
3. Add a test issuing over-threshold reset attempts while rotating X-Forwarded-For and asserting HTTP 429.

**Verification:** Send 150 POSTs to `/rest/user/reset-password` from one source with a unique X-Forwarded-For value each time and assert requests beyond the threshold return HTTP 429.

<a id="m-035"></a>
#### M-035 — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-035](#f-035) — Coupon discount unbounded server-side (`chat.ts:184`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing `Authorization`

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**How:**

1. Constrain the tool schema `at ``routes/chat.ts:179` to `z.number().int().min(1).max(10)` and re-check the bound inside the executor before calling `security.generateCoupon` `at ``routes/chat.ts:184`.
2. Resolve the caller from a signature-verified token and verify server-side that the claimed damaged order exists and belongs to them before any coupon is issued.
3. Add a test driving a tool call with discount 100 and asserting the executor rejects it and no coupon code appears in the stream.

_Example implementation in `routes/chat.ts:184`: it applies **Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Add server-side role check on every admin route.
router.use('/admin/*', (req, res, next) => {
  if (req.user?.role !== 'admin') return res.status(403).end()
  next()
})
```

**Verification:** Invoke the `generateCoupon` executor with discount 100 in the test harness and assert it throws or returns an error, and that `security.generateCoupon` is never called.

**Reference:** OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications 2025 - LLM06: Excessive Agency

3. Add a test posting `paymentMode: 'free'` and asserting HTTP 400 and an unchanged `user` `role`.

<a id="m-037"></a>
#### M-037 — Require and verify the current password

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-037](#f-037) — Unverified password change (`changePassword.ts:39`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-620](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**How:**

1. Reject the request with 401 when `currentPassword` is absent `at ``routes/changePassword.ts:39`, then compare it against `loggedInUser.data.password` unconditionally.
2. Invalidate the caller's other sessions after a successful change by removing the corresponding `authenticatedUsers` entries.
3. Add a test asserting that a change-password request without a current parameter returns 401 and leaves the stored `password` unchanged.

**Verification:** Run the new test: a change-password request with a valid token but no current parameter returns HTTP 401 and the persisted `password` `hash` is byte-identical before and after.

<a id="m-038"></a>
#### M-038 — Replace security-answer recovery with an emailed single-use reset token

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-038](#f-038) — Security-question password recovery (`resetPassword.ts:41`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) - Weak Password Recovery Mechanism

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** High · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Replace the answer comparison `at ``routes/resetPassword.ts:41` with verification of a short-lived single-use token generated by `crypto.randomBytes` and delivered only to the registered address, never returned in the response body or URL.
2. Keep the response non-enumerating so an unknown email and a known email produce the same status and timing, and apply the shared-store rate limiter from the login remediation to this route.
3. Add tests asserting the reset response body and logs contain no token, that a token is single-use, and that it expires.

**Verification:** Run the new tests: the reset-request response body contains no token value, replaying a consumed token returns 401, and a token used after its TTL returns 401.

**Reference:** [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html): Weak Password Recovery Mechanism for Forgotten Password

<a id="m-039"></a>
#### M-039 — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-039](#f-039) — Comment command without author authorization (`rebase.yml:10`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing `Authorization`

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/rebase.yml:10`

**How:**

1. Extend the condition `at ``.github/workflows/rebase.yml:10` to require `contains(fromJSON('["OWNER","MEMBER","COLLABORATOR"]'), github.event.comment.author_association)` so only privileged commenters trigger the job.
2. Add an explicit `permissions: contents: write` block to the rebase job so the token is not issued with the repository default scope.
3. Add a concurrency group keyed on the issue number so repeated `/rebase` comments cannot queue parallel rebases of the same branch.

_Example implementation in `.github/workflows/rebase.yml:10`: it applies **Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Add server-side role check on every admin route.
router.use('/admin/*', (req, res, next) => {
  if (req.user?.role !== 'admin') return res.status(403).end()
  next()
})
```

**Verification:** Comment `/rebase` from an account with no repository permission and confirm the rebase job is skipped while the workflow run itself still completes via `always_job`.

**How:**

1. Delete the confidential clause `at ``routes/chat.ts:105` from the prompt and implement the courtesy-discount decision as a server-side rule the model can request through a tool but cannot read.
2. Treat the remaining prompt as public text and review it for any other internal criteria before release.
3. Add a test that sends a 'repeat your `system` instructions' message and asserts the streamed response contains neither the string 'CONFIDENTIAL' nor '15%'.

_Example implementation in `routes/chat.ts:105`: it applies **Stop exposing internal information to clients**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Remove directory-listing middleware; require auth on management endpoints.
// app.use('/ftp', serveIndex(...))   // delete
app.use('/metrics', requireRole('admin'), promBundle())
```

**Verification:** POST `/rest/chat` asking the bot to repeat its instructions verbatim and assert the concatenated SSE text contains no occurrence of 'CONFIDENTIAL' or '15%'.

**Reference:** OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications 2025 - LLM07: System Prompt Leakage

<a id="m-062"></a>
#### M-062 — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-062](#f-062) — Unbounded session token map (`insecurity.ts:74`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) - Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:74`

**How:**

1. Replace the plain object `at ``lib/insecurity.ts:74` with a bounded LRU or TTL cache whose entry lifetime matches the 6h token expiry, and delete entries on logout and on `password` change.
2. Cap the map size and reject insertion beyond the cap rather than growing without limit.
3. Add a test that inserts more entries than the configured cap and asserts the map size stays at or below the cap and that an expired entry is no longer resolvable.

**Verification:** Run the new test: after inserting cap+1 tokens the map reports at most cap entries, and a token advanced past its TTL resolves to undefined.

**Reference:** [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html): Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

<a id="m-063"></a>
#### M-063 — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-063](#f-063) — Unbounded CI fan-out on pull requests (`ci.yml:14`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) - Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`

**How:**

1. Add a workflow-level `concurrency: { group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}, cancel-in-progress: true }` block to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` so a superseded pull request run is cancelled.
2. Reduce the `pull_request` matrix to a single operating `system` and the default Node version, keeping the full three-by-three matrix for push events on master and develop.
3. Enable the repository setting that requires approval before workflows run for first-time contributors, so an unknown account cannot queue runners without a maintainer action.

**Verification:** Push two commits to a pull request branch within a few seconds and confirm the Actions run list shows the first run cancelled rather than completed.

**Reference:** [GitHub Docs: Workflow Syntax For Github Actions](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/writing-workflows/workflow-syntax-for-github-actions#concurrency)

<a id="m-064"></a>
#### M-064 — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-064](#f-064) — Unbounded regex on socket input (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:46`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) - Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:46`

**How:**

1. Set an explicit `maxHttpBufferSize` on the Socket\.IO server options `at ``lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`, sized to the largest legitimate verification payload rather than the default megabyte.
2. Reject oversized or non-string payloads at the top of each `socket.on` handler before any matching runs, and apply a per-socket event rate limit that disconnects a socket exceeding it.
3. Rewrite the regex at line 46 to remove the leading `.*` and the lazy quantifier, anchoring it and matching the path components explicitly so match cost stays linear in input length.
4. Add a load test that emits oversized `verifySvgInjectionChallenge` payloads at high rate and asserts REST endpoint latency on the same port stays within budget.

**Verification:** Emit 200 `verifySvgInjectionChallenge` events of 1 MB each from a script while polling `GET /rest/admin/application-version`; `median` response latency must stay under 200 ms and oversized payloads must be rejected.

**Reference:** [CWE-400: Uncontrolled Resource Consumption](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html)

<a id="m-067"></a>
#### M-067 — Apply least-privilege filesystem access

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-067](#f-067) — Default-scope workflow token (`release.yml:20`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html) - Improper Access Control

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:20`

**How:**

1. Add a top-level `permissions: contents: read` to `.github/workflows/release.yml` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, then grant `contents: write` only on the create-release and package jobs that attach artifacts.
2. Apply the same top-level read-only default to `image_actions.yml`, `lint-fixer.yml`, `rebase.yml`, `frontend-bundle-analysis.yml`, `zap_scan.yml`, `stale.yml` and the five update-* workflows, raising individual jobs only where they demonstrably need write.
3. Set the repository's default `GITHUB_TOKEN` workflow permissions to read-only in the Actions settings so a workflow that omits the block fails closed.

_Example implementation in `.github/workflows/release.yml:20`: it applies **Apply least-privilege filesystem access**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Centralize access decisions in a single middleware.
function requireRole(role: 'admin' | 'user') {
  return (req, res, next) =>
    req.user?.role === role ? next() : res.status(403).end()
}
```

**Verification:** Push a test tag and confirm the release run still attaches the packaged archives while a step running `gh api repos/:owner/:repo/issues -f title=probe` fails with 403.

**Reference:** [GitHub Docs: Automatic Token Authentication](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-for-github-actions/security-guides/automatic-token-authentication#permissions-for-the-github_token)

## 11. Out of Scope

### Not Covered by This Method

This is a code-derived threat model at implementation level: it is built from source, configuration and git history, models the `system` as built rather than as designed, and does not `replace` a design-time review.

- Design intent and the reasoning behind it - no design documents, ADRs or workshop context are read.
- Business processes and `user` journeys that leave the code.
- Runtime behaviour, deployment topology and production-only configuration.
- External and organizational controls.

Treat this report as review input, not sign-off.

### Excluded from This Assessment

The following items are **explicitly excluded** from this threat model. Findings against these areas should be tracked separately.

- Third-party hosted dependencies and SaaS endpoints
- Browser runtime vulnerabilities and end-user device security
- Operating `system` kernel and container runtime
- Underlying network infrastructure (DNS, BGP, ISP)
- Physical security of hosting facilities

<a id="appendix-a-vektor-taxonomy"></a>
## Appendix A — Vektor Taxonomy

This appendix defines the attacker-starting-position labels used in the Top Threats table and throughout [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register). Each label answers the question *what does the attacker need before the exploit begins?*

<a id="vektor-internet-anon"></a>
### Internet Anon

**Attacker position:** Unauthenticated attacker from the public internet · **Breach distance:** 1

**Preconditions:**

- Endpoint is reachable from the internet (no IP allowlist, no VPN)
- No authentication middleware blocks the request

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-user"></a>
### Internet User

**Attacker position:** Any authenticated low-privilege `user` (valid JWT / session) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker has signed up or otherwise obtained a valid `user` session
- Endpoint is behind auth but not behind role/admin checks

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A06:2025, A02:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-priv-user"></a>
### Internet Priv User

**Attacker position:** Authenticated admin-level `user` (JWT with admin `role` or equivalent) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker holds admin credentials or has elevated privileges
- Endpoint gated on admin `role` but still exploitable once reached

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025

<a id="vektor-victim-required"></a>
### Victim-Required

**Attacker position:** Attacker needs victim interaction - social engineering, crafted link, or live session · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Victim must click a link, load a page, or have an active session
- Applies to XSS, CSRF, click-jacking, open redirect

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [CWE-1021](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1021.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025

<a id="vektor-build-time"></a>
### Build-Time

**Attacker position:** Attacker controls a build input - CI runner, dependency, base image, or external `data` fetched during build · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Compromise of a dependency, registry, or base image
- OR compromise of a CI runner with write access to artifacts

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [CWE-1039](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1039.html) · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A08:2025

<a id="vektor-repo-read"></a>
### Repo-Read

**Attacker position:** Attacker gains read access to source repository (leaked clone, forked fork, insider, compromised developer workstation) · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Read access to the source tree at or after commit time
- No runtime exploit needed - the vulnerability is the content of the repo

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [CWE-540](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/540.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A04:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-n-a"></a>
### n/a

**Attacker position:** Architectural / meta-finding - no runtime entry point, the finding describes a design defect aggregating multiple code-level findings

**Preconditions:**

- Finding is AF-NNN (architectural) rather than F-NNN (code-level)
